  "content": "# Cycle protocol\n\nEvery change runs as a **cycle**: Preparation → Work-N →\nClose-out. One protocol; push and squash are actions you take\nwhen wanted, not modes chosen up front. The mechanics below\nrun in the order they happen.\n\n## Numbering\n\nThe version suffix on each commit carries its phase, with one\nseparator — `.` — at every depth (semver mandates `-` once at\nthe start of the prerelease, then `.` for each level after):\n\n- `X.Y.Z-0` — Preparation\n- `X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, … — Work commits\n- `X.Y.Z` — Close-out (bare version, no suffix)\n\nA **trailing `0` identifier marks a Preparation**, and the\nrule nests to any depth: when a Work commit needs subdividing\ninto its own sub-cycle, append another level — `X.Y.Z-3.0`\n(Preparation of the `-3` sub-cycle), `X.Y.Z-3.1` /\n`X.Y.Z-3.2` (its Work), `X.Y.Z-3` (its Close-out),\n`X.Y.Z-3.1.0` (Preparation of the `-3.1` sub-sub-cycle), and\nso on. A sub-cycle needing no Preparation just omits the `.0`\n(`-3.1`, `-3.2`, `-3`); one that grows a Preparation later\nadds `-3.0` with no renumbering of siblings.\n\nEach commit carries its phase version in its title **and**\n`Cargo.toml` — bump `Cargo.toml` at the start of each phase,\nso `vc-x1 -V` shows the active phase at build time.\n\n**Ordering caveat.** The one ordering guarantee semver gives\nis that the bare `X.Y.Z` close-out outranks every `X.Y.Z-…`\ndev version. Within the ladder it does *not* hold for nested\nclose-outs — `X.Y.Z-3` sorts *before* its children\n`X.Y.Z-3.0` / `X.Y.Z-3.1`, because a prefix always precedes\nits extensions. Cosmetic — nothing depends on within-ladder\norder — but don't claim monotonicity.\n\n**Why numeric suffixes** (`-0`, `-1`, …) rather than `-devN`:\nsemver pre-release identifiers compare per spec and Cargo\naccepts them. `-dev` adds no information the git log doesn't\ncarry and doubles typing per commit. The final close-out\ncommit (bare `X.Y.Z`) signals completion rather than amending\nprior commits — history stays readable, exploratory vs final\ncommits obvious.\n\n## Preparation\n\nThe cycle's first commit (`X.Y.Z-0`):\n\n- **Backfill the previous cycle's chores `Commits:` ref.**\n  Grab the just-pushed commit's URL + full SHA, fill in the\n  newest chores section that was left `Commits:`-less. See\n  [Chores commit references](../CLAUDE.md#chores-commit-references).\n- **Bump the version** in `Cargo.toml` to `X.Y.Z-0`.\n- **Pick up a `## Todo` item** (if the cycle has one). Move\n  that entry's text out of `## Todo` and into `## In\n  Progress` — a **bold title line** (exact match for the\n  chores section header, minus the `## ` prefix) +\n  **succinct problem statement** + **plan ladder**. See\n  [Prose form](../CLAUDE.md#prose-form) (Problem + plan shape).\n  A cycle that merges several entangled `## Todo` items moves\n  all of them. A `## Todo` entry that duplicates current\n  `## In Progress` work is a process bug.\n- **Open the chores section.** Header = the cycle's\n  **anticipated close-out title** (e.g. `## refactor: foo\n  bar (X.Y.Z)`). No body — the cycle's live narrative\n  lives in `## In Progress` and moves into the section at\n  close-out per the \"title only holds position\" rule. One\n  chores section per cycle is the default; per-commit\n  sections (like 0.55.0's four) are a deliberate choice.\n\n## Per-commit flow\n\nEvery commit (Preparation, each Work commit, Close-out) goes\nthrough:\n\n1. **Mark this commit `(current)`** as the first edit in\n   `notes/todo.md > ## In Progress`, before any code/doc\n   work.\n2. **Do the work.**\n3. **Flip this commit `(current)` → `(done)`** in `## In\n   Progress` — before the cargo cycle and the commit, so\n   the commit captures the completed state.\n4. **Cargo cycle** (skip-able for purely-docs commits; full\n   cycle mandatory at close-out):\n   1. `cargo fmt`\n   2. `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`\n   3. `cargo test`\n   4. `cargo install --path . --locked`\n   5. (re-test if anything substantive changed)\n5. **Work review.** Stop *before* writing any description;\n   tell the user \"ready to commit.\" The user reviews the\n   working-copy changes in their editor. Most change\n   requests come here — iterate until the work is right.\n6. **Write the commit description.** [Conventional\n   Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/) with a\n   version suffix:\n\n   ```\n   <type>: <short description> (<version>)\n   ```\n\n   - **Title**: ≤50 chars. Common types: `feat`, `fix`,\n     `refactor`, `test`, `docs`, `chore`. The version\n     suffix counts toward the 50 — favor terse phrasings.\n   - **Body**: [Prose form](../CLAUDE.md#prose-form) (intro\n     + bullets), wrap ≤72. App-repo bullets are\n     **file-by-file** — one bullet per file changed, file\n     plus a one-line gist. When a file has several distinct\n     changes, list them as `-` sub-bullets under the file\n     bullet.\n\n     ```\n     - path/to/file1\n       - first distinct change\n       - second distinct change, wrapping to next line\n          with continuation indented 5\n     ```\n\n     The file-by-file list is the source of truth for the\n     cycle's mechanical change record; chores carries the\n     narrative + design, not a copy of it. Promote any\n     \"why\" beyond one sentence to a chores `###`\n     subsection.\n   - **`ochid:` trailer** as the last line of the body —\n     see [ochid trailers](#ochid-trailers).\n   - **Session-repo (`.claude`) body**: same shape; bullets\n     describe in-session activity rather than code changes.\n7. **Commit Description review.** Show the title + body\n   and stop. The user reviews the description. Iterate.\n8. **Commit.** `jj commit -m \"title\" -m \"body\" -R .` for\n   the app repo, `-R .claude` for the bot repo (`-R` last\n   keeps the verb visible):\n\n   ```\n   jj commit -m \\\n   \"<type>: <short description> (<version>)\" \\\n   -m \"<intro paragraph>\n\n   - file1: gist\n   - file2: gist\n\n   ochid: /.claude/<chid>\" \\\n   -R .\n   ```\n\n**Two overrides apply:**\n\n- **Deviation or question** — any time the work deviates\n  from the agreed plan, or a question arises, stop and\n  surface it; don't push through.\n- **ESC-ESC** — the user can interrupt at any point to pull\n  a review or question forward.\n\n## Reviewing changes\n\nWork review looks at **uncommitted working-copy changes**\nin your editor, on the way to commit. Zed shows the diff\nnaturally; for terminal use:\n\n```\njj diff                          # working-copy diff (uncommitted)\njj diff -r @-                    # diff of the previous commit\njj diff --from <X> --to <Y>      # any two revisions\njj show -r <X>                   # description + diff for one rev\n```\n\nPipe through `delta` or `diff-so-fancy | less -R` for color\nand side-by-side. To launch your editor as the diff tool:\n\n```\n# ~/.config/jj/config.toml\n[ui]\ndiff-editor = [\"zed\", \"--diff\", \"$left\", \"$right\"]\n```\n\nThen `jj diff --from X --to Y` opens it. Don't `jj edit\n-r @-` to view a past commit — that marks it mutable and\nshifts the working-copy pointer; use `jj diff -r @-` or\n`jj show -r @-`.\n\n[Sub-cycles](#sub-cycles) below covers the close-out\nsquash recipe and recovery for ladders. Revset primitives\n(`@`, `@-`, `..`, `::`, prefix matching) are in\n[`jj-revsets.md`](jj-revsets.md).\n\n## Close-out\n\nThe cycle's last commit (bare `X.Y.Z`):\n\n- **Move the picked-up item** from `## In Progress` to a\n  one-line entry (with a chores `[N]` ref) in `## Done`.\n- **Move the `## In Progress` block into the chores\n  section.** Cut the problem statement + plan ladder out of\n  `## In Progress` (drop the bold title line — the chores\n  `##` header carries the same string) and paste them\n  under the bare chores `##` header (the title-only\n  placeholder from Preparation). Sync the chores header to\n  the **final** commit title if the cycle's scope shifted,\n  and update every markdown anchor back-reference to it.\n  Add an `### As-built ladder` listing the cycle's\n  commits; `### Outcome` notes if useful. Replace\n  `## In Progress` with `_No cycle currently in progress._`.\n- **Update `notes/README.md`** if functionality changed\n  (new flags, new subcommands, changed behavior).\n\nThe close-out commit's body should describe **only what's\nin that commit** (the bookkeeping: clearing `## In\nProgress`, the `## Done` entry, chores section finalize)\n— not what would happen post-squash. Squash / merge /\nkeep is a separate push-time decision (Post close-out\nreview, see [Pushing](#pushing) below); keeping the\nclose-out body shape-neutral avoids locking in\nsquash-language before the decision is made.\n\nWhether to **squash** the cycle into one commit before the\npublishing push, or push as-is, is decided at push time —\nsee [Pushing](#pushing).\n\n## Pushing\n\n**Push is discretionary** — done when you want to back up\nwork or publish interim progress, and **always at\nclose-out** (the cycle's result must be published). Interim\npushes are a judgment call; nothing forces push-per-commit.\n\n**Post close-out review (close-out push only).** After\nthe close-out commit and before any `jj squash` / `jj\nrebase` / `jj git push` invocation, stop and ask the user\nwhich shape the cycle lands as (squash / merge / keep)\nand to approve the push. The choice changes the commits\non the target and is hard to undo cleanly once pushed;\ntreat it like the Work and Commit Description reviews —\nsurface the options, wait for the user, don't auto-pick.\nESC-ESC and the deviation override apply as usual.\n\n**Squash, merge, or keep at the close-out push.** Before\nthe publishing push, decide what shape the cycle lands as:\n\n- **Squash to one commit** — single entry on the target;\n  per-commit granularity preserved only in the commit\n  body's edit list. Right when the work is one logical\n  change with intermediate validation points. Cost: the\n  per-commit chores sections collapse into one (its header\n  becomes the close-out commit title), and every anchor\n  back-ref to a now-gone section gets re-pointed — real\n  work, done as part of the squash.\n- **Merge (non-FF)** — `main` gains one merge commit\n  (`X.Y.Z`) with parents `<prev>` and the cycle tip; the\n  sub-step commits stay reachable as the merge's second\n  parent. `git log` shows everything; `git log\n  --first-parent` shows main as one jump per cycle. Best\n  when the sub-steps are worth preserving but main should\n  read linearly. The paired `.claude` commit's body\n  carries a multi-line ochid listing every app commit in\n  the push — the multi-ochid case\n  [`forks-multi-user.md`](forks-multi-user.md) designs.\n  Set up with `jj rebase -r <tip> -d <prev> -d <sub-tip>`\n  before invoking `jj git push`.\n- **Keep separate** — one commit per cycle entry on main —\n  when the decomposition is itself informative and you\n  want `git log` to show every step. Each chores section\n  keeps its own header / `Commits:` ref; no consolidation\n  churn.\n\nIf squashing or merging, do it before invoking `vc-x1\npush` (or push manually with `jj git push` for\nnon-standard shapes).\n\nThe flow is wrapped by `vc-x1 push <bookmark>`, which runs:\n\n1. Preflight (`vc-x1 sync --check` + `cargo fmt` / `clippy`\n   / `test`).\n2. **Work-review prompt** — shows `jj diff --stat` for\n   both repos and asks `[y/N]`.\n3. **Commit-description-review prompt** — uses `--title`\n   / `--body` or opens `$EDITOR`.\n4. Commits both repos with ochid trailers, advances the\n   bookmark, `jj git push --bookmark <bookmark>` for the\n   app, finalizes `.claude` (its single per-push commit).\n\n`<bookmark>` is the **app-side working branch** (often\n`main`, sometimes a feature branch). `.claude` always pushes\nits `main` regardless of the app-side bookmark. See\n`vc-x1 push --help` for the full flag list, resumability\n(`--from`, `--restart`, `--status`), and recovery; CLAUDE.md\nkeeps only the rules that govern bot behavior. `vc-x1 sync\n--check` may also be run manually before editing to surface\nremote changes early.\n\n**`.claude` cadence — once per push.** The `.claude`\nworking-copy node accumulates session data across the\ncycle's commits and is described + committed when a push\nhappens — one push, one `.claude` commit, paired with every\ncode commit in that push. Its change ID is stable from the\nfirst code commit that references it through to that\n`.claude` commit (working-copy snapshots, `jj describe`, and\nthe finalize `jj commit` all preserve the change ID), so the\n`ochid:` trailers resolve across the push.\n\n`.claude` is a linear journal — all session work lives on\n`main`. The repo has no need for parallel feature-branch\nbookmarks to mirror app-side branches; when the app sits on\na feature branch, `.claude` still commits to `main`.\nCross-references between sides are carried by the `ochid:`\ntrailer + commit timestamps; that is enough. **Do not\ncreate or maintain `.claude` bookmarks that mirror app-side\nbranches** — tried during 0.41.1-6.7, went unused, and\nrisks the bot steering session pushes to the wrong remote\nref.\n\n**Bot communication at the reviews.** Use plain prose —\nno insider jargon (\"Gate N signal\", \"Checkpoint N\", etc.):\n\n- **At Work review** — summarize what changed and stop.\n  \"Work complete. Please review.\"\n- **At Commit Description review** — present `$TITLE` and\n  `$BODY` explicitly, then ask permission to commit (or\n  push). Don't spell out the full `vc-x1 push <bookmark>\n  --yes --title \"$TITLE\" --body \"$BODY\"` invocation by\n  default; it's mechanical and obvious from title/body.\n  Show it only on explicit request.\n- **At Post close-out review** — surface the shape\n  options (squash / merge / keep) with a brief trade-off\n  summary, plus the push target; wait for the user's\n  choice before any `jj squash` / `jj rebase` / `jj git\n  push` invocation.\n\n**After finalize: stop and wait.** After `vc-x1 finalize`\nlaunches — whether mid-session per-push or at session-end —\nyou **MUST NEVER** proceed to a next step, edit files, run\ntools, or emit any text (prose, recaps, acknowledgements),\nuntil the user explicitly directs you to continue. Treat\nfinalize as a hard stop for the whole turn. Any final words\nmust be said in the approval prompt *before* executing\nfinalize; the finalize `Bash` call is the last thing in the\nturn and nothing follows it.\n\nThis holds even when the next step seems obvious. Wait. The\nuser controls cadence — every push+finalize is a checkpoint\nthey may want to inspect, think about, hand off, or take a\nbreak at. Auto-proceeding bypasses that checkpoint.\n\nIf push exits before `finalize-claude` (e.g. failure between\n`push-app` and `finalize-claude`), run finalize by hand:\n\n```\nvc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log\n```\n\nClear push's saved state after any out-of-band recovery —\n`rm .vc-x1/push-state.toml` or `vc-x1 push <bookmark>\n--restart` — otherwise push resumes from a stale stage.\n\nA late code-repo tweak after `push-app` succeeded (e.g.\nupdating CLAUDE.md or memory) requires `jj squash\n--ignore-immutable` and a re-push; that is a remote rewrite\nand needs explicit approval like any push.\n\n## ochid trailers\n\nEvery commit body carries `ochid:` trailer(s) linking it to\nits counterpart(s) in the other repo. The path is\nworkspace-root-relative — `/.claude/<chid>` from the app\nside, `/<chid>` from the `.claude` side.\n\nBecause `.claude` commits once per push:\n\n- **Code-side commits** each carry one\n  `ochid: /.claude/<.claude-chid>` — the `.claude`\n  working-copy change ID, stable until `.claude` is\n  committed at push time.\n- **The `.claude` commit** carries one `ochid: /<code-chid>`\n  per code commit in that push: a single trailer when one\n  code commit is pushed (the squashed case), a list when\n  several are. The multi-line `ochid:` list and its\n  fork / multi-user generalization are designed in\n  [`forks-multi-user.md`](forks-multi-user.md).\n\nUse `vc-x1 chid -s code,bot -L` to capture the change IDs\n(first line app, second `.claude`).\n\n## Sub-cycles\n\nA Work commit `X.Y.Z-N` may need subdividing into its own\nsub-cycle — `X.Y.Z-N.0` Preparation, `X.Y.Z-N.1` /\n`X.Y.Z-N.2` Work, `X.Y.Z-N` Close-out. See\n[Numbering](#numbering) for the suffix rules.\n\nThe local ladder of sub-cycle Work commits is typically\nkept on its own jj `@` chain and **collapsed into the\nsub-cycle's Close-out** before the parent cycle continues.\nSub-cycle ladder commits are scratch — they exist for\nreview and bisection during the sub-cycle and disappear\ninto one commit at sub-cycle close-out.\n\n### Per-Work-commit contract within a ladder\n\nFor each Work commit in the ladder:\n\n1. `jj new -R .` — create a fresh empty `@`.\n2. Do the commit's work on this `@`.\n3. Run `cargo test --bins`. **Non-negotiable.** Build and\n   clippy alone are not enough — past experience\n   (0.41.1-6.5) had a regression introduced in an early\n   ladder commit that didn't surface until a later one ran\n   the full suite, by which point bisection cost was higher\n   than it needed to be.\n4. `jj describe -m \"...\" -m \"...\" -R .` — give it a working\n   title and body. The title does **not** need a version\n   suffix during the ladder; the sub-cycle Close-out collects\n   every commit into one final commit carrying the\n   `(X.Y.Z-N)` marker. Ladder titles are scratch — they\n   never reach the remote.\n\n### Navigating the ladder\n\nCommon moves while a ladder is active:\n\n- `jj log -r 'all()' -R .` — see the whole ladder at a\n  glance.\n- `jj edit -r <prefix> -R .` — jump `@` to any commit in\n  the ladder by chid prefix. Useful for bisection: re-run\n  the failing test at each candidate without manual\n  checkout juggling.\n- `jj edit @-- -R .` — quick-jump back two commits.\n- `jj diff -r <chid> -R .` — review one commit's diff in\n  isolation.\n\nExample using a revset with the `::` operator from the last\ncommit pushed to the remote:\n\n```\n$ jj log -r lu::\n○  pynknoqu wink@saville.com 2026-05-01 16:16:23 33612c81\n│  docs: cycle protocol pointer\n@  xqpnxtyu wink@saville.com 2026-05-01 16:16:23 f40c49a7\n│  docs: cycle protocol formal protocol\n○  lzqmlpvy wink@saville.com 2026-05-01 16:16:23 8c722f8c\n│  docs: jj-revsets.md review fixes\n○  wmmxkuoz wink@saville.com 2026-05-01 11:28:46 65a6705f\n│  chore: scratch — jj-revsets learning + scaffolding\n◆  lulqxovr wink@saville.com 2026-04-27 22:27:23 main 55eadc8e\n│  docs: capture 0.41.1 init+clone redesign plan (0.42.0-4)\n~\n```\n\nLadder work happens on whatever `@` is currently selected.\nUse `jj edit -r <chid>` to switch to whichever ladder\ncommit you want to work on. Modifications to a selected\ncommit rewrite that commit in place; descendants auto-rebase.\n\n### Close-out: squash the ladder\n\nWhen all ladder Work commits are done and `cargo test --bins`\nis green on the latest:\n\n```\njj squash --from \"<base>..@-\" --into @ -u -R .\n```\n\nWhere `<base>` is the parent of the **first** ladder\ncommit — typically the bookmark or commit the ladder\nbranched from. The `-u` flag (use destination message)\ntells jj to keep `@`'s description and discard the source\ndescriptions; the intermediate titles served their purpose,\nand `vc-x1 push` will overwrite `@`'s description anyway.\n\nAfter squash:\n\n- All ladder diffs are folded into `@`.\n- The intermediate commits become empty and are\n  abandoned automatically.\n- History is linear: `<base> → @`.\n\nThen run `vc-x1 push <bookmark>` exactly as for any other\ncommit. Push's `commit-app` stage finalizes `@` with the\nsub-cycle Close-out title/body and the proper `(X.Y.Z-N)`\nversion suffix.\n\n**Validation status.** The recipe was validated 2026-05-01\nagainst scratch ladders built by\n[`substep-test.sh`](substep-test.sh):\n\n- 3-commit ladder (`base..@-` = {c1, c2}, `@ = c3`):\n  squash produces `base → @` linear; intermediate commits\n  abandoned; `@` retains its description.\n- 5-commit ladder (`base..@-` = {c1..c4}, `@ = c5`): same\n  outcome at scale.\n\nThe recipe is N-agnostic for N ≥ 2 ladder commits. For\nN = 1 the close-out squash is a no-op (`base..@-` is empty\nwhen `@-` is `<base>`); push the single commit directly.\n\n### Recovery\n\nIf a ladder commit goes wrong and you want to back out\nwithout losing prior commits:\n\n- **Discard the current commit's work.** `jj abandon @ -R .`\n  drops it and gives you a fresh empty `@` on the same\n  parent. Earlier commits are untouched.\n- **Edit an earlier commit.** `jj edit -r <chid> -R .`,\n  make corrections, then `jj edit -r <last-ladder-chid>`\n  to return. Descendants auto-rebase.\n- **Discard the entire ladder.** `jj op log -R .` shows the\n  op history; `jj op restore <op-id> -R .` reverts the repo\n  to that point. This is a full undo — it removes *all*\n  ladder work after the chosen op. Use only when you want\n  to start over. **Not** a close-out recipe; the squash\n  above is.\n\n### Worked example\n\nA ladder of 3 commits starting from bookmark `main` at\ncommit `abc1234`:\n\n```\njj new -R .                                # commit 1\n... edit files, cargo test --bins ...\njj describe -m \"commit 1: ...\" -R .\n\njj new -R .                                # commit 2\n... edit files, cargo test --bins ...\njj describe -m \"commit 2: ...\" -R .\n\njj new -R .                                # commit 3\n... edit files, cargo test --bins ...\njj describe -m \"commit 3: ...\" -R .\n```\n\n`jj log -r 'all()'` now shows:\n\n```\n@  yyy ... commit 3: ...\n○  xxx ... commit 2: ...\n○  www ... commit 1: ...\n○  abc1234 main | <previous commit>\n```\n\nClose out:\n\n```\njj squash --from \"main..@-\" --into @ -u -R .\n```\n\nResult:\n\n```\n@  yyy ... commit 3: ...   (now contains all ladder diffs)\n○  abc1234 main | <previous commit>\n```\n\nThen `vc-x1 push main --title \"...\" --body \"...\"` finalizes\nthe sub-cycle.\n\n**Note:** Above you see `-R .` being used to explicitly select\nthe current repo. For the bot the explicitness is desirable;\nfor others it's optional.\n\n# References\n\n- [`jj-revsets.md`](jj-revsets.md) — revset primitives\n  (chid/cid, `@`/`@-`/`@+`, `..`/`::` ranges, prefix matching).\n- [`forks-multi-user.md`](forks-multi-user.md) — multi-ochid\n  trailer design (fork / multi-user generalization).\n- [`substep-test.sh`](substep-test.sh) — script that\n  scaffolds a 4-revision ladder under `/tmp/substep-test`\n  for squash-recipe experiments.\n- 0.41.1-6.5 cycle — first multi-commit ladder usage. The\n  per-commit `cargo test --bins` gate originated there\n  after a regression introduced in an early commit wasn't\n  caught until a later one ran the full suite.\n"
}
```

Within the ladder it does *not* hold for nested\nclose-outs — `X.Y.Z-3` sorts *before* its children\n`X.Y.Z-3.0` / `X.Y.Z-3.1`, because a prefix always precedes\nits extensions. Cosmetic — nothing depends on within-ladder\norder — but don't claim monotonicity.\n\n**Why numeric suffixes** (`-0`, `-1`, …) rather than `-devN`:\nsemver pre-release identifiers compare per spec and Cargo\naccepts them. `-dev` adds no information the git log doesn't\ncarry and doubles typing per commit. The final close-out\ncommit (bare `X.Y.Z`) signals completion rather than amending\nprior commits — history stays readable, exploratory vs final\ncommits obvious.\n\n## Preparation",

466	
467	**Validation status.** The recipe was validated 2026-05-01
468	against scratch ladders built by
469	[`substep-test.sh`](substep-test.sh):
470	
471	- 3-commit ladder (`base..@-` = {c1, c2}, `@ = c3`):
472	  squash produces `base → @` linear; intermediate commits
473	  abandoned; `@` retains its description.
474	- 5-commit ladder (`base..@-` = {c1..c4}, `@ = c5`): same
475	  outcome at scale.
476	
477	The recipe is N-agnostic for N ≥ 2 ladder commits. For
478	N = 1 the close-out squash is a no-op (`base..@-` is empty
479	when `@-` is `<base>`); push the single commit directly.
480	
481	### Recovery
482	
483	If a ladder commit goes wrong and you want to back out
484	without losing prior commits:
485	
486	- **Discard the current commit's work.** `jj abandon @ -R .`
487	  drops it and gives you a fresh empty `@` on the same
488	  parent. Earlier commits are untouched.
489	- **Edit an earlier commit.** `jj edit -r <chid> -R .`,
490	  make corrections, then `jj edit -r <last-ladder-chid>`
491	  to return. Descendants auto-rebase.
492	- **Discard the entire ladder.** `jj op log -R .` shows the
493	  op history; `jj op restore <op-id> -R .` reverts the repo
494	  to that point. This is a full undo — it removes *all*
495	  ladder work after the chosen op. Use only when you want
496	  to start over. **Not** a close-out recipe; the squash
497	  above is.
498	
499	### Worked example
500	
501	A ladder of 3 commits starting from bookmark `main` at
502	commit `abc1234`:
503	
504	```
505	jj new -R .                                # commit 1
506	... edit files, cargo test --bins ...
507	jj describe -m "commit 1: ..." -R .
508	
509	jj new -R .                                # commit 2
510	... edit files, cargo test --bins ...
511	jj describe -m "commit 2: ..." -R .
512	
513	jj new -R .                                # commit 3
514	... edit files, cargo test --bins ...
515	jj describe -m "commit 3: ..." -R .
516	```
517	
518	`jj log -r 'all()'` now shows:
519	
520	```
521	@  yyy ... commit 3: ...
522	○  xxx ... commit 2: ...
523	○  www ... commit 1: ...
524	○  abc1234 main | <previous commit>
525	```
526	
527	Close out:
528	
529	```
530	jj squash --from "main..@-" --into @ -u -R .
531	```
532	
533	Result:
534	
535	```
536	@  yyy ... commit 3: ...   (now contains all ladder diffs)
537	○  abc1234 main | <previous commit>
538	```
539	
540	Then `vc-x1 push main --title "..." --body "..."` finalizes
541	the sub-cycle.
542	
543	**Note:** Above you see `-R .` being used to explicitly select
544	the current repo. For the bot the explicitness is desirable;
545	for others it's optional.
546	
547	# References
```

```
285	`ochid:` trailers resolve across the push.
286	
287	`.claude` is a linear journal — all session work lives on
288	`main`. The repo has no need for parallel feature-branch
289	bookmarks to mirror app-side branches; when the app sits on
290	a feature branch, `.claude` still commits to `main`.
291	Cross-references between sides are carried by the `ochid:`
292	trailer + commit timestamps; that is enough. **Do not
293	create or maintain `.claude` bookmarks that mirror app-side
294	branches** — tried during 0.41.1-6.7, went unused, and
295	risks the bot steering session pushes to the wrong remote
296	ref.
297	
298	**Bot communication at the reviews.** Use plain prose —
299	no insider jargon ("Gate N signal", "Checkpoint N", etc.):
300	
301	- **At Work review** — summarize what changed and stop.
302	  "Work complete. Please review."
303	- **At Commit Description review** — present `$TITLE` and
304	  `$BODY` explicitly, then ask permission to commit (or
305	  push). Don't spell out the full `vc-x1 push <bookmark>
306	  --yes --title "$TITLE" --body "$BODY"` invocation by
307	  default; it's mechanical and obvious from title/body.
308	  Show it only on explicit request.
309	- **At Post close-out review** — surface the shape
310	  options (squash / merge / keep) with a brief trade-off
311	  summary, plus the push target; wait for the user's
312	  choice before any `jj squash` / `jj rebase` / `jj git
313	  push` invocation.
314	
315	**After finalize: stop and wait.** After `vc-x1 finalize`
316	launches — whether mid-session per-push or at session-end —
317	you **MUST NEVER** proceed to a next step, edit files, run
318	tools, or emit any text (prose, recaps, acknowledgements),
319	until the user explicitly directs you to continue. Treat
320	finalize as a hard stop for the whole turn. Any final words
321	must be said in the approval prompt *before* executing
322	finalize; the finalize `Bash` call is the last thing in the
323	turn and nothing follows it.
324	
325	This holds even when the next step seems obvious. Wait. The
326	user controls cadence — every push+finalize is a checkpoint
327	they may want to inspect, think about, hand off, or take a
328	break at. Auto-proceeding bypasses that checkpoint.
329	
330	If push exits before `finalize-claude` (e.g. failure between
331	`push-app` and `finalize-claude`), run finalize by hand:
332	
333	```
334	vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
```

I thinking ## Sub-cycles should be the first '##' header and maybe it would be

> AGENT

Yes, that structure reads better — overview-first, details-later. Here's what I'd draft for the new `## Cycles` section as the first major heading:

```
## Cycles

A cycle has three phases:

- **Preparation** (`X.Y.Z-0`) — the cycle's first commit.
  Sets up the cycle by bumping the version, picking up a
  `## Todo` item into `## In Progress`, and opening the
  chores section header. Operational steps:
  [Preparation](#preparation).
- **Work-N** (`X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, …) — the work commits
  that implement the change. As many as the change needs;
  each runs through the [per-commit flow](#per-commit-flow).
- **Close-out** (bare `X.Y.Z`) — the cycle's last commit.
  Bookkeeping only: moves `## In Progress` into the chores
  section, adds a `## Done` entry, optionally updates
  `notes/README.md`. Operational steps: [Close-out](#close-out).

A cycle's commits are published to the project remote
either incrementally or as one batch at close-out; the
result must always be published at close-out. See
[Pushing](#pushing).

**Sub-cycles.** When a Work commit's scope grows enough to
warrant its own ladder, it subdivides — `X.Y.Z-3.0`
Preparation, `X.Y.Z-3.1` / `X.Y.Z-3.2` Work, `X.Y.Z-3`
Close-out. The same three-phase shape applies recursively
at every depth. See [Numbering](#numbering) for the
suffix rule and [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders)
for ladder mechanics.
```

Plus: drop the file-level intro paragraph (lines 3-6); rename the existing `## Sub-cycles` (at the bottom) to `## Sub-cycle ladders` and drop its intro about sub-cycle nesting (now covered in `## Cycles`).

**One choice worth settling first**: do `## Preparation` and `## Close-out` stay as their own operational sections (the overview points at them via links), or do they fold into `## Cycles` as `### Preparation` / `### Close-out` subsections? Tradeoff:

- **Keep separate** (recommended): `## Cycles` stays a tight overview (~25 lines); operational detail lives in dedicated `##` sections. Small duplication between overview bullets and section content, but layers are clear.
- **Fold in**: single `## Cycles` section per-phase with subsections holds both definition + operations. Fewer top-level sections, no duplication, but `## Cycles` grows to ~80 lines and starts looking like the old all-in-one block.

Which way?

> DEVELOPER

1	# Cycle protocol
2	
3	Every change runs as a **cycle**: Preparation → Work-N →
4	Close-out. One protocol; push and squash are actions you take
5	when wanted, not modes chosen up front. The mechanics below
6	run in the order they happen.
7	
8	## Numbering
9	
10	The version suffix on each commit carries its phase, with one
11	separator — `.` — at every depth (semver mandates `-` once at
12	the start of the prerelease, then `.` for each level after):
13	
14	- `X.Y.Z-0` — Preparation
15	- `X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, … — Work commits
16	- `X.Y.Z` — Close-out (bare version, no suffix)
17	
18	A **trailing `0` identifier marks a Preparation**, and the
19	rule nests to any depth: when a Work commit needs subdividing
20	into its own sub-cycle, append another level — `X.Y.Z-3.0`
21	(Preparation of the `-3` sub-cycle), `X.Y.Z-3.1` /
22	`X.Y.Z-3.2` (its Work), `X.Y.Z-3` (its Close-out),
23	`X.Y.Z-3.1.0` (Preparation of the `-3.1` sub-sub-cycle), and
24	so on. A sub-cycle needing no Preparation just omits the `.0`
25	(`-3.1`, `-3.2`, `-3`); one that grows a Preparation later
26	adds `-3.0` with no renumbering of siblings.
27	
28	Each commit carries its phase version in its title **and**
29	`Cargo.toml` — bump `Cargo.toml` at the start of each phase,
30	so `vc-x1 -V` shows the active phase at build time.
31	
32	## Preparation
33	
34	The cycle's first commit (`X.Y.Z-0`):
35	
36	- **Backfill the previous cycle's chores `Commits:` ref.**
37	  Grab the just-pushed commit's URL + full SHA, fill in the
38	  newest chores section that was left `Commits:`-less. See
39	  [Chores commit references](../CLAUDE.md#chores-commit-references).
40	- **Bump the version** in `Cargo.toml` to `X.Y.Z-0`.
41	- **Pick up a `## Todo` item** (if the cycle has one). Move
42	  that entry's text out of `## Todo` and into `## In
43	  Progress` — a **bold title line** (exact match for the
44	  chores section header, minus the `## ` prefix) +
45	  **succinct problem statement** + **plan ladder**. See
46	  [Prose form](../CLAUDE.md#prose-form) (Problem + plan shape).
47	  A cycle that merges several entangled `## Todo` items moves
48	  all of them. A `## Todo` entry that duplicates current
49	  `## In Progress` work is a process bug.
50	- **Open the chores section.** Header = the cycle's
51	  **anticipated close-out title** (e.g. `## refactor: foo
52	  bar (X.Y.Z)`). No body — the cycle's live narrative
53	  lives in `## In Progress` and moves into the section at
54	  close-out per the "title only holds position" rule. One
55	  chores section per cycle is the default; per-commit
56	  sections (like 0.55.0's four) are a deliberate choice.
57	
58	## Per-commit flow
59	
60	Every commit (Preparation, each Work commit, Close-out) goes
61	through:
62	
63	1. **Mark this commit `(current)`** as the first edit in
64	   `notes/todo.md > ## In Progress`, before any code/doc
65	   work.
66	2. **Do the work.**
67	3. **Flip this commit `(current)` → `(done)`** in `## In
68	   Progress` — before the cargo cycle and the commit, so
69	   the commit captures the completed state.
70	4. **Cargo cycle** (skip-able for purely-docs commits; full
71	   cycle mandatory at close-out):
72	   1. `cargo fmt`
73	   2. `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`
74	   3. `cargo test`
75	   4. `cargo install --path . --locked`
76	   5. (re-test if anything substantive changed)
77	5. **Work review.** Stop *before* writing any description;
78	   tell the user "ready to commit." The user reviews the
79	   working-copy changes in their editor. Most change
80	   requests come here — iterate until the work is right.
81	6. **Write the commit description.** [Conventional
82	   Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/) with a
83	   version suffix:
84	
85	   ```
86	   <type>: <short description> (<version>)
87	   ```
88	
89	   - **Title**: ≤50 chars. Common types: `feat`, `fix`,
90	     `refactor`, `test`, `docs`, `chore`. The version
91	     suffix counts toward the 50 — favor terse phrasings.
92	   - **Body**: [Prose form](../CLAUDE.md#prose-form) (intro
93	     + bullets), wrap ≤72. App-repo bullets are
94	     **file-by-file** — one bullet per file changed, file
95	     plus a one-line gist. When a file has several distinct
96	     changes, list them as `-` sub-bullets under the file
97	     bullet.
98	
99	     ```
100	     - path/to/file1
101	       - first distinct change
102	       - second distinct change, wrapping to next line
103	          with continuation indented 5
104	     ```
105	
106	     The file-by-file list is the source of truth for the
107	     cycle's mechanical change record; chores carries the
108	     narrative + design, not a copy of it. Promote any
109	     "why" beyond one sentence to a chores `###`
110	     subsection.
111	   - **`ochid:` trailer** as the last line of the body —
112	     see [ochid trailers](#ochid-trailers).
113	   - **Session-repo (`.claude`) body**: same shape; bullets
114	     describe in-session activity rather than code changes.
115	7. **Commit Description review.** Show the title + body
116	   and stop. The user reviews the description. Iterate.
117	8. **Commit.** `jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R .` for
118	   the app repo, `-R .claude` for the bot repo (`-R` last
119	   keeps the verb visible):
120	
121	   ```
122	   jj commit -m \
123	   "<type>: <short description> (<version>)" \
124	   -m "<intro paragraph>
125	
126	   - file1: gist
127	   - file2: gist
128	
129	   ochid: /.claude/<chid>" \
130	   -R .
131	   ```
132	
133	**Two overrides apply:**
134	
135	- **Deviation or question** — any time the work deviates
136	  from the agreed plan, or a question arises, stop and
137	  surface it; don't push through.
138	- **ESC-ESC** — the user can interrupt at any point to pull
139	  a review or question forward.
140	
141	## Reviewing changes
142	
143	Work review looks at **uncommitted working-copy changes**
144	in your editor, on the way to commit. Zed shows the diff
145	naturally; for terminal use:
146	
147	```
148	jj diff                          # working-copy diff (uncommitted)
149	jj diff -r @-                    # diff of the previous commit
150	jj diff --from <X> --to <Y>      # any two revisions
151	jj show -r <X>                   # description + diff for one rev
152	```
153	
154	Pipe through `delta` or `diff-so-fancy | less -R` for color
155	and side-by-side. To launch your editor as the diff tool:
156	
157	```
158	# ~/.config/jj/config.toml
159	[ui]
160	diff-editor = ["zed", "--diff", "$left", "$right"]
161	```
162	
163	Then `jj diff --from X --to Y` opens it. Don't `jj edit
164	-r @-` to view a past commit — that marks it mutable and
165	shifts the working-copy pointer; use `jj diff -r @-` or
166	`jj show -r @-`.
167	
168	[Sub-cycles](#sub-cycles) below covers the close-out
169	squash recipe and recovery for ladders. Revset primitives
170	(`@`, `@-`, `..`, `::`, prefix matching) are in
171	[`jj-revsets.md`](jj-revsets.md).
172	
173	## Close-out
174	
175	The cycle's last commit (bare `X.Y.Z`):
176	
177	- **Move the picked-up item** from `## In Progress` to a
178	  one-line entry (with a chores `[N]` ref) in `## Done`.
179	- **Move the `## In Progress` block into the chores
180	  section.** Cut the problem statement + plan ladder out of
181	  `## In Progress` (drop the bold title line — the chores
182	  `##` header carries the same string) and paste them
183	  under the bare chores `##` header (the title-only
184	  placeholder from Preparation). Sync the chores header to
185	  the **final** commit title if the cycle's scope shifted,
186	  and update every markdown anchor back-reference to it.
187	  Add an `### As-built ladder` listing the cycle's
188	  commits; `### Outcome` notes if useful. Replace
189	  `## In Progress` with `_No cycle currently in progress._`.
190	- **Update `notes/README.md`** if functionality changed
191	  (new flags, new subcommands, changed behavior).
192	
193	The close-out commit's body should describe **only what's
194	in that commit** (the bookkeeping: clearing `## In
195	Progress`, the `## Done` entry, chores section finalize)
196	— not what would happen post-squash. Squash / merge /
197	keep is a separate push-time decision (Post close-out
198	review, see [Pushing](#pushing) below); keeping the
199	close-out body shape-neutral avoids locking in
200	squash-language before the decision is made.
201	
202	Whether to **squash** the cycle into one commit before the
203	publishing push, or push as-is, is decided at push time —
204	see [Pushing](#pushing).
205	
206	## Pushing
207	
208	**Push is discretionary** — done when you want to back up
209	work or publish interim progress, and **always at
210	close-out** (the cycle's result must be published). Interim
211	pushes are a judgment call; nothing forces push-per-commit.
212	
213	**Post close-out review (close-out push only).** After
214	the close-out commit and before any `jj squash` / `jj
215	rebase` / `jj git push` invocation, stop and ask the user
216	which shape the cycle lands as (squash / merge / keep)
217	and to approve the push. The choice changes the commits
218	on the target and is hard to undo cleanly once pushed;
219	treat it like the Work and Commit Description reviews —
220	surface the options, wait for the user, don't auto-pick.
221	ESC-ESC and the deviation override apply as usual.
222	
223	**Squash, merge, or keep at the close-out push.** Before
224	the publishing push, decide what shape the cycle lands as:
225	
226	- **Squash to one commit** — single entry on the target;
227	  per-commit granularity preserved only in the commit
228	  body's edit list. Right when the work is one logical
229	  change with intermediate validation points. Cost: the
230	  per-commit chores sections collapse into one (its header
231	  becomes the close-out commit title), and every anchor
232	  back-ref to a now-gone section gets re-pointed — real
233	  work, done as part of the squash.
234	- **Merge (non-FF)** — `main` gains one merge commit
235	  (`X.Y.Z`) with parents `<prev>` and the cycle tip; the
236	  sub-step commits stay reachable as the merge's second
237	  parent. `git log` shows everything; `git log
238	  --first-parent` shows main as one jump per cycle. Best
239	  when the sub-steps are worth preserving but main should
240	  read linearly. The paired `.claude` commit's body
241	  carries a multi-line ochid listing every app commit in
242	  the push — the multi-ochid case
243	  [`forks-multi-user.md`](forks-multi-user.md) designs.
244	  Set up with `jj rebase -r <tip> -d <prev> -d <sub-tip>`
245	  before invoking `jj git push`.
246	- **Keep separate** — one commit per cycle entry on main —
247	  when the decomposition is itself informative and you
248	  want `git log` to show every step. Each chores section
249	  keeps its own header / `Commits:` ref; no consolidation
250	  churn.
251	
252	If squashing or merging, do it before invoking `vc-x1
253	push` (or push manually with `jj git push` for
254	non-standard shapes).
255	
256	The flow is wrapped by `vc-x1 push <bookmark>`, which runs:
257	
258	1. Preflight (`vc-x1 sync --check` + `cargo fmt` / `clippy`
259	   / `test`).
260	2. **Work-review prompt** — shows `jj diff --stat` for
261	   both repos and asks `[y/N]`.
262	3. **Commit-description-review prompt** — uses `--title`
263	   / `--body` or opens `$EDITOR`.
264	4. Commits both repos with ochid trailers, advances the
265	   bookmark, `jj git push --bookmark <bookmark>` for the
266	   app, finalizes `.claude` (its single per-push commit).
267	
268	`<bookmark>` is the **app-side working branch** (often
269	`main`, sometimes a feature branch). `.claude` always pushes
270	its `main` regardless of the app-side bookmark. See
271	`vc-x1 push --help` for the full flag list, resumability
272	(`--from`, `--restart`, `--status`), and recovery; CLAUDE.md
273	keeps only the rules that govern bot behavior. `vc-x1 sync
274	--check` may also be run manually before editing to surface
275	remote changes early.
276	
277	**`.claude` cadence — once per push.** The `.claude`
278	working-copy node accumulates session data across the
279	cycle's commits and is described + committed when a push
280	happens — one push, one `.claude` commit, paired with every
281	code commit in that push. Its change ID is stable from the
282	first code commit that references it through to that
283	`.claude` commit (working-copy snapshots, `jj describe`, and
284	the finalize `jj commit` all preserve the change ID), so the
285	`ochid:` trailers resolve across the push.
286	
287	`.claude` is a linear journal — all session work lives on
288	`main`, regardless of the app-side bookmark. **Do not
289	create or maintain `.claude` bookmarks that mirror app-side
290	branches** — risks the bot steering session pushes to the
291	wrong remote ref.
292	
293	**Bot communication at the reviews.** Use plain prose —
294	no insider jargon ("Gate N signal", "Checkpoint N", etc.):
295	
296	- **At Work review** — summarize what changed and stop.
297	  "Work complete. Please review."
298	- **At Commit Description review** — present `$TITLE` and
299	  `$BODY` explicitly, then ask permission to commit (or
300	  push). Don't spell out the full `vc-x1 push <bookmark>
301	  --yes --title "$TITLE" --body "$BODY"` invocation by
302	  default; it's mechanical and obvious from title/body.
303	  Show it only on explicit request.
304	- **At Post close-out review** — surface the shape
305	  options (squash / merge / keep) with a brief trade-off
306	  summary, plus the push target; wait for the user's
307	  choice before any `jj squash` / `jj rebase` / `jj git
308	  push` invocation.
309	
310	**After finalize: stop and wait.** After `vc-x1 finalize`
311	launches — whether mid-session per-push or at session-end —
312	you **MUST NEVER** proceed to a next step, edit files, run
313	tools, or emit any text until the user explicitly directs
314	you to continue. Treat finalize as a hard stop for the
315	whole turn; any final words go in the approval prompt
316	*before* executing finalize. The user controls cadence —
317	auto-proceeding bypasses the push+finalize checkpoint.
318	
319	If push exits before `finalize-claude` (e.g. failure between
320	`push-app` and `finalize-claude`), run finalize by hand:
321	
322	```
323	vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
324	```
325	
326	Clear push's saved state after any out-of-band recovery —
327	`rm .vc-x1/push-state.toml` or `vc-x1 push <bookmark>
328	--restart` — otherwise push resumes from a stale stage.
329	
330	A late code-repo tweak after `push-app` succeeded (e.g.
331	updating CLAUDE.md or memory) requires `jj squash
332	--ignore-immutable` and a re-push; that is a remote rewrite
333	and needs explicit approval like any push.
334	
335	## ochid trailers
336	
337	Every commit body carries `ochid:` trailer(s) linking it to
338	its counterpart(s) in the other repo. The path is
339	workspace-root-relative — `/.claude/<chid>` from the app
340	side, `/<chid>` from the `.claude` side.
341	
342	Because `.claude` commits once per push:
343	
344	- **Code-side commits** each carry one
345	  `ochid: /.claude/<.claude-chid>` — the `.claude`
346	  working-copy change ID, stable until `.claude` is
347	  committed at push time.
348	- **The `.claude` commit** carries one `ochid: /<code-chid>`
349	  per code commit in that push: a single trailer when one
350	  code commit is pushed (the squashed case), a list when
351	  several are. The multi-line `ochid:` list and its
352	  fork / multi-user generalization are designed in
353	  [`forks-multi-user.md`](forks-multi-user.md).
354	
355	Use `vc-x1 chid -s code,bot -L` to capture the change IDs
356	(first line app, second `.claude`).
357	
358	## Sub-cycles
359	
360	A Work commit `X.Y.Z-N` may need subdividing into its own
361	sub-cycle — `X.Y.Z-N.0` Preparation, `X.Y.Z-N.1` /
362	`X.Y.Z-N.2` Work, `X.Y.Z-N` Close-out. See
363	[Numbering](#numbering) for the suffix rules.
364	
365	The local ladder of sub-cycle Work commits is typically
366	kept on its own jj `@` chain and **collapsed into the
367	sub-cycle's Close-out** before the parent cycle continues.
368	Sub-cycle ladder commits are scratch — they exist for
369	review and bisection during the sub-cycle and disappear
370	into one commit at sub-cycle close-out.
371	
372	### Per-Work-commit contract within a ladder
373	
374	For each Work commit in the ladder:
375	
376	1. `jj new -R .` — create a fresh empty `@`.
377	2. Do the commit's work on this `@`.
378	3. Run `cargo test --bins`. **Non-negotiable.** Build and
379	   clippy alone are not enough — past experience
380	   (0.41.1-6.5) had a regression introduced in an early
381	   ladder commit that didn't surface until a later one ran
382	   the full suite, by which point bisection cost was higher
383	   than it needed to be.
384	4. `jj describe -m "..." -m "..." -R .` — give it a working
385	   title and body. The title does **not** need a version
386	   suffix during the ladder; the sub-cycle Close-out collects
387	   every commit into one final commit carrying the
388	   `(X.Y.Z-N)` marker. Ladder titles are scratch — they
389	   never reach the remote.
390	
391	### Navigating the ladder
392	
393	Common moves while a ladder is active:
394	
395	- `jj log -r 'all()' -R .` — see the whole ladder at a
396	  glance.
397	- `jj edit -r <prefix> -R .` — jump `@` to any commit in
398	  the ladder by chid prefix. Useful for bisection: re-run
399	  the failing test at each candidate without manual
400	  checkout juggling.
401	- `jj edit @-- -R .` — quick-jump back two commits.
402	- `jj diff -r <chid> -R .` — review one commit's diff in
403	  isolation.
404	
405	Example using a revset with the `::` operator from the last
406	commit pushed to the remote:
407	
408	```
409	$ jj log -r lu::
410	○  pynknoqu wink@saville.com 2026-05-01 16:16:23 33612c81
411	│  docs: cycle protocol pointer
412	@  xqpnxtyu wink@saville.com 2026-05-01 16:16:23 f40c49a7
413	│  docs: cycle protocol formal protocol
414	○  lzqmlpvy wink@saville.com 2026-05-01 16:16:23 8c722f8c
415	│  docs: jj-revsets.md review fixes
416	○  wmmxkuoz wink@saville.com 2026-05-01 11:28:46 65a6705f
417	│  chore: scratch — jj-revsets learning + scaffolding
418	◆  lulqxovr wink@saville.com 2026-04-27 22:27:23 main 55eadc8e
419	│  docs: capture 0.41.1 init+clone redesign plan (0.42.0-4)
420	~
421	```
422	
423	Ladder work happens on whatever `@` is currently selected.
424	Use `jj edit -r <chid>` to switch to whichever ladder
425	commit you want to work on. Modifications to a selected
426	commit rewrite that commit in place; descendants auto-rebase.
427	
428	### Close-out: squash the ladder
429	
430	When all ladder Work commits are done and `cargo test --bins`
431	is green on the latest:
432	
433	```
434	jj squash --from "<base>..@-" --into @ -u -R .
435	```
436	
437	Where `<base>` is the parent of the **first** ladder
438	commit — typically the bookmark or commit the ladder
439	branched from. The `-u` flag (use destination message)
440	tells jj to keep `@`'s description and discard the source
441	descriptions; the intermediate titles served their purpose,
442	and `vc-x1 push` will overwrite `@`'s description anyway.
443	
444	After squash:
445	
446	- All ladder diffs are folded into `@`.
447	- The intermediate commits become empty and are
448	  abandoned automatically.
449	- History is linear: `<base> → @`.
450	
451	Then run `vc-x1 push <bookmark>` exactly as for any other
452	commit. Push's `commit-app` stage finalizes `@` with the
453	sub-cycle Close-out title/body and the proper `(X.Y.Z-N)`
454	version suffix.
455	
456	For N = 1 the close-out squash is a no-op (`base..@-` is
457	empty when `@-` is `<base>`); push the single commit
458	directly.
459	
460	### Recovery
461	
462	If a ladder commit goes wrong and you want to back out
463	without losing prior commits:
464	
465	- **Discard the current commit's work.** `jj abandon @ -R .`
466	  drops it and gives you a fresh empty `@` on the same
467	  parent. Earlier commits are untouched.
468	- **Edit an earlier commit.** `jj edit -r <chid> -R .`,
469	  make corrections, then `jj edit -r <last-ladder-chid>`
470	  to return. Descendants auto-rebase.
471	- **Discard the entire ladder.** `jj op log -R .` shows the
472	  op history; `jj op restore <op-id> -R .` reverts the repo
473	  to that point. This is a full undo — it removes *all*
474	  ladder work after the chosen op. Use only when you want
475	  to start over. **Not** a close-out recipe; the squash
476	  above is.
477	
478	# References
479	
480	- [`jj-revsets.md`](jj-revsets.md) — revset primitives
481	  (chid/cid, `@`/`@-`/`@+`, `..`/`::` ranges, prefix matching).
482	- [`forks-multi-user.md`](forks-multi-user.md) — multi-ochid
483	  trailer design (fork / multi-user generalization).
484	- [`substep-test.sh`](substep-test.sh) — script that
485	  scaffolds a 4-revision ladder under `/tmp/substep-test`
486	  for squash-recipe experiments.
487	- 0.41.1-6.5 cycle — first multi-commit ladder usage. The
488	  per-commit `cargo test --bins` gate originated there
489	  after a regression introduced in an early commit wasn't
490	  caught until a later one ran the full suite.
491	
```

{

  "content": "# Cycle protocol\n\n## Cycles\n\nA cycle has three phases:\n\n- **Preparation** (`X.Y.Z-0`) — the cycle's first commit.\n  Sets up the cycle by bumping the version, picking up a\n  `## Todo` item into `## In Progress`, and opening the\n  chores section header. As many as the change needs;\n  each runs through the [per-commit flow](#per-commit-flow).\n  Operational notes: [Work-N](#work-n).\n- **Close-out** (bare `X.Y.Z`) — the cycle's last commit.\n  Bookkeeping only: moves `## In Progress` into the chores\n  section, adds a `## Done` entry, optionally updates\n  `notes/README.md`. Operational steps: [Close-out](#close-out).\n\nA cycle's commits are published to the project remote\neither incrementally or as one batch at close-out; the\nresult must always be published at close-out. See\n[Pushing](#pushing).\n\n**Sub-cycles.** When a Work commit's scope grows enough to\nwarrant its own ladder, it subdivides — `X.Y.Z-3.0`\nPreparation, `X.Y.Z-3.1` / `X.Y.Z-3.2` Work, `X.Y.Z-3`\nClose-out. The same three-phase shape applies recursively\nat every depth. See [Numbering](#numbering) for the\nsuffix rule and [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders)\nfor the local-ladder mechanics.\n\n## Preparation\n\nThe cycle's first commit (`X.Y.Z-0`):\n\n- **Backfill the previous cycle's chores `Commits:` ref.**\n  Grab the just-pushed commit's URL + full SHA, fill in the\n  newest chores section that was left `Commits:`-less. See\n  [Chores commit references](../CLAUDE.md#chores-commit-references).\n- **Bump the version** in `Cargo.toml` to `X.Y.Z-0`.\n- **Pick up a `## Todo` item** (if the cycle has one). Move\n  that entry's text out of `## Todo` and into `## In\n  Progress` — a **bold title line** (exact match for the\n  chores section header, minus the `## ` prefix) +\n  **succinct problem statement** + **plan ladder**. See\n  [Prose form](../CLAUDE.md#prose-form) (Problem + plan shape).\n  A cycle that merges several entangled `## Todo` items moves\n  all of them. A `## Todo` entry that duplicates current\n  `## In Progress` work is a process bug.\n- **Open the chores section.** Header = the cycle's\n  **anticipated close-out title** (e.g. `## refactor: foo\n  bar (X.Y.Z)`). No body — the cycle's live narrative\n  lives in `## In Progress` and moves into the section at\n  close-out per the \"title only holds position\" rule. One\n  chores section per cycle is the default; per-commit\n  sections (like 0.55.0's four) are a deliberate choice.\n\n## Work-N\n\nThe cycle's work commits implement the change. Number as\nmany `X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, … as the change needs; each\nruns through the [per-commit flow](#per-commit-flow).\nInterim pushes are optional (backup, progress visibility);\nclose-out is the only mandatory push — see\n[Pushing](#pushing). For sub-cycles see\n[Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders).\n\n## Close-out\n\nThe cycle's last commit (bare `X.Y.Z`):\n\n- **Move the picked-up item** from `## In Progress` to a\n  one-line entry (with a chores `[N]` ref) in `## Done`.\n- **Move the `## In Progress` block into the chores\n  section.** Cut the problem statement + plan ladder out of\n  `## In Progress` (drop the bold title line — the chores\n  `##` header carries the same string) and paste them\n  under the bare chores `##` header (the title-only\n  placeholder from Preparation). Sync the chores header to\n  the **final** commit title if the cycle's scope shifted,\n  and update every markdown anchor back-reference to it.\n  Add an `### As-built ladder` listing the cycle's\n  commits; `### Outcome` notes if useful. Replace\n  `## In Progress` with `_No cycle currently in progress._`.\n- **Update `notes/README.md`** if functionality changed\n  (new flags, new subcommands, changed behavior).\n\nThe close-out commit's body should describe **only what's\nin that commit** (the bookkeeping: clearing `## In\nProgress`, the `## Done` entry, chores section finalize)\n— not what would happen post-squash. Squash / merge /\nkeep is a separate push-time decision (Post close-out\nreview, see [Pushing](#pushing) below); keeping the\nclose-out body shape-neutral avoids locking in\nsquash-language before the decision is made.\n\nWhether to **squash** the cycle into one commit before the\npublishing push, or push as-is, is decided at push time —\nsee [Pushing](#pushing).\n\n## Numbering\n\nThe version suffix on each commit carries its phase, with one\nseparator — `.` — at every depth (semver mandates `-` once at\nthe start of the prerelease, then `.` for each level after):\n\n- `X.Y.Z-0` — Preparation\n- `X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, … — Work commits\n- `X.Y.Z` — Close-out (bare version, no suffix)\n\nA **trailing `0` identifier marks a Preparation**, and the\nrule nests to any depth: when a Work commit needs subdividing\ninto its own sub-cycle, append another level — `X.Y.Z-3.0`\n(Preparation of the `-3` sub-cycle), `X.Y.Z-3.1` /\n`X.Y.Z-3.2` (its Work), `X.Y.Z-3` (its Close-out),\n`X.Y.Z-3.1.0` (Preparation of the `-3.1` sub-sub-cycle), and\nso on. A sub-cycle needing no Preparation just omits the `.0`\n(`-3.1`, `-3.2`, `-3`); one that grows a Preparation later\nadds `-3.0` with no renumbering of siblings.\n\nEach commit carries its phase version in its title **and**\n`Cargo.toml` — bump `Cargo.toml` at the start of each phase,\nso `vc-x1 -V` shows the active phase at build time.\n\n## Per-commit flow\n\nEvery commit (Preparation, each Work commit, Close-out) goes\nthrough:\n\n1. **Mark this commit `(current)`** as the first edit in\n   `notes/todo.md > ## In Progress`, before any code/doc\n   work.\n2. **Do the work.**\n3. **Flip this commit `(current)` → `(done)`** in `## In\n   Progress` — before the cargo cycle and the commit, so\n   the commit captures the completed state.\n4. **Cargo cycle** (skip-able for purely-docs commits; full\n   cycle mandatory at close-out):\n   1. `cargo fmt`\n   2. `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`\n   3. `cargo test`\n   4. `cargo install --path . --locked`\n   5. (re-test if anything substantive changed)\n5. **Work review.** Stop *before* writing any description;\n   tell the user \"ready to commit.\" The user reviews the\n   working-copy changes in their editor. Most change\n   requests come here — iterate until the work is right.\n6. **Write the commit description.** [Conventional\n   Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/) with a\n   version suffix:\n\n   ```\n   <type>: <short description> (<version>)\n   ```\n\n   - **Title**: ≤50 chars. Common types: `feat`, `fix`,\n     `refactor`, `test`, `docs`, `chore`. The version\n     suffix counts toward the 50 — favor terse phrasings.\n   - **Body**: [Prose form](../CLAUDE.md#prose-form) (intro\n     + bullets), wrap ≤72. App-repo bullets are\n     **file-by-file** — one bullet per file changed, file\n     plus a one-line gist. When a file has several distinct\n     changes, list them as `-` sub-bullets under the file\n     bullet.\n\n     ```\n     - path/to/file1\n       - first distinct change\n       - second distinct change, wrapping to next line\n          with continuation indented 5\n     ```\n\n     The file-by-file list is the source of truth for the\n     cycle's mechanical change record; chores carries the\n     narrative + design, not a copy of it. Promote any\n     \"why\" beyond one sentence to a chores `###`\n     subsection.\n   - **`ochid:` trailer** as the last line of the body —\n     see [ochid trailers](#ochid-trailers).\n   - **Session-repo (`.claude`) body**: same shape; bullets\n     describe in-session activity rather than code changes.\n7. **Commit Description review.** Show the title + body\n   and stop. The user reviews the description. Iterate.\n8. **Commit.** `jj commit -m \"title\" -m \"body\" -R .` for\n   the app repo, `-R .claude` for the bot repo (`-R` last\n   keeps the verb visible):\n\n   ```\n   jj commit -m \\\n   \"<type>: <short description> (<version>)\" \\\n   -m \"<intro paragraph>\n\n   - file1: gist\n   - file2: gist\n\n   ochid: /.claude/<chid>\" \\\n   -R .\n   ```\n\n**Two overrides apply:**\n\n- **Deviation or question** — any time the work deviates\n  from the agreed plan, or a question arises, stop and\n  surface it; don't push through.\n- **ESC-ESC** — the user can interrupt at any point to pull\n  a review or question forward.\n\n## Reviewing changes\n\nWork review looks at **uncommitted working-copy changes**\nin your editor, on the way to commit. Zed shows the diff\nnaturally; for terminal use:\n\n```\njj diff                          # working-copy diff (uncommitted)\njj diff -r @-                    # diff of the previous commit\njj diff --from <X> --to <Y>      # any two revisions\njj show -r <X>                   # description + diff for one rev\n```\n\nPipe through `delta` or `diff-so-fancy | less -R` for color\nand side-by-side. To launch your editor as the diff tool:\n\n```\n# ~/.config/jj/config.toml\n[ui]\ndiff-editor = [\"zed\", \"--diff\", \"$left\", \"$right\"]\n```\n\nThen `jj diff --from X --to Y` opens it. Don't `jj edit\n-r @-` to view a past commit — that marks it mutable and\nshifts the working-copy pointer; use `jj diff -r @-` or\n`jj show -r @-`.\n\n[Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders) below covers the\nclose-out squash recipe and recovery for ladders. Revset\nprimitives (`@`, `@-`, `..`, `::`, prefix matching) are in\n[`jj-revsets.md`](jj-revsets.md).\n\n## Pushing\n\n**Push is discretionary** — done when you want to back up\nwork or publish interim progress, and **always at\nclose-out** (the cycle's result must be published). Interim\npushes are a judgment call; nothing forces push-per-commit.\n\n**Post close-out review (close-out push only).** After\nthe close-out commit and before any `jj squash` / `jj\nrebase` / `jj git push` invocation, stop and ask the user\nwhich shape the cycle lands as (squash / merge / keep)\nand to approve the push. The choice changes the commits\non the target and is hard to undo cleanly once pushed;\ntreat it like the Work and Commit Description reviews —\nsurface the options, wait for the user, don't auto-pick.\nESC-ESC and the deviation override apply as usual.\n\n**Squash, merge, or keep at the close-out push.** Before\nthe publishing push, decide what shape the cycle lands as:\n\n- **Squash to one commit** — single entry on the target;\n  per-commit granularity preserved only in the commit\n  body's edit list. Right when the work is one logical\n  change with intermediate validation points. Cost: the\n  per-commit chores sections collapse into one (its header\n  becomes the close-out commit title), and every anchor\n  back-ref to a now-gone section gets re-pointed — real\n  work, done as part of the squash.\n- **Merge (non-FF)** — `main` gains one merge commit\n  (`X.Y.Z`) with parents `<prev>` and the cycle tip; the\n  sub-step commits stay reachable as the merge's second\n  parent. `git log` shows everything; `git log\n  --first-parent` shows main as one jump per cycle. Best\n  when the sub-steps are worth preserving but main should\n  read linearly. The paired `.claude` commit's body\n  carries a multi-line ochid listing every app commit in\n  the push — the multi-ochid case\n  [`forks-multi-user.md`](forks-multi-user.md) designs.\n  Set up with `jj rebase -r <tip> -d <prev> -d <sub-tip>`\n  before invoking `jj git push`.\n- **Keep separate** — one commit per cycle entry on main —\n  when the decomposition is itself informative and you\n  want `git log` to show every step. Each chores section\n  keeps its own header / `Commits:` ref; no consolidation\n  churn.\n\nIf squashing or merging, do it before invoking `vc-x1\npush` (or push manually with `jj git push` for\nnon-standard shapes).\n\nThe flow is wrapped by `vc-x1 push <bookmark>`, which runs:\n\n1. Preflight (`vc-x1 sync --check` + `cargo fmt` / `clippy`\n   / `test`).\n2. **Work-review prompt** — shows `jj diff --stat` for\n   both repos and asks `[y/N]`.\n3. **Commit-description-review prompt** — uses `--title`\n   / `--body` or opens `$EDITOR`.\n4. Commits both repos with ochid trailers, advances the\n   bookmark, `jj git push --bookmark <bookmark>` for the\n   app, finalizes `.claude` (its single per-push commit).\n\n`<bookmark>` is the **app-side working branch** (often\n`main`, sometimes a feature branch). `.claude` always pushes\nits `main` regardless of the app-side bookmark. See\n`vc-x1 push --help` for the full flag list, resumability\n(`--from`, `--restart`, `--status`), and recovery; CLAUDE.md\nkeeps only the rules that govern bot behavior. `vc-x1 sync\n--check` may also be run manually before editing to surface\nremote changes early.\n\n**`.claude` cadence — once per push.** The `.claude`\nworking-copy node accumulates session data across the\ncycle's commits and is described + committed when a push\nhappens — one push, one `.claude` commit, paired with every\ncode commit in that push. Its change ID is stable from the\nfirst code commit that references it through to that\n`.claude` commit (working-copy snapshots, `jj describe`, and\nthe finalize `jj commit` all preserve the change ID), so the\n`ochid:` trailers resolve across the push.\n\n`.claude` is a linear journal — all session work lives on\n`main`, regardless of the app-side bookmark. **Do not\ncreate or maintain `.claude` bookmarks that mirror app-side\nbranches** — risks the bot steering session pushes to the\nwrong remote ref.\n\n**Bot communication at the reviews.** Use plain prose —\nno insider jargon (\"Gate N signal\", \"Checkpoint N\", etc.):\n\n- **At Work review** — summarize what changed and stop.\n  \"Work complete. Please review.\"\n- **At Commit Description review** — present `$TITLE` and\n  `$BODY` explicitly, then ask permission to commit (or\n  push). Don't spell out the full `vc-x1 push <bookmark>\n  --yes --title \"$TITLE\" --body \"$BODY\"` invocation by\n  default; it's mechanical and obvious from title/body.\n  Show it only on explicit request.\n- **At Post close-out review** — surface the shape\n  options (squash / merge / keep) with a brief trade-off\n  summary, plus the push target; wait for the user's\n  choice before any `jj squash` / `jj rebase` / `jj git\n  push` invocation.\n\n**After finalize: stop and wait.** After `vc-x1 finalize`\nlaunches — whether mid-session per-push or at session-end —\nyou **MUST NEVER** proceed to a next step, edit files, run\ntools, or emit any text until the user explicitly directs\nyou to continue. Treat finalize as a hard stop for the\nwhole turn; any final words go in the approval prompt\n*before* executing finalize. The user controls cadence —\nauto-proceeding bypasses the push+finalize checkpoint.\n\nIf push exits before `finalize-claude` (e.g. failure between\n`push-app` and `finalize-claude`), run finalize by hand:\n\n```\nvc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log\n```\n\nClear push's saved state after any out-of-band recovery —\n`rm .vc-x1/push-state.toml` or `vc-x1 push <bookmark>\n--restart` — otherwise push resumes from a stale stage.\n\nA late code-repo tweak after `push-app` succeeded (e.g.\nupdating CLAUDE.md or memory) requires `jj squash\n--ignore-immutable` and a re-push; that is a remote rewrite\nand needs explicit approval like any push.\n\n## ochid trailers\n\nEvery commit body carries `ochid:` trailer(s) linking it to\nits counterpart(s) in the other repo. The path is\nworkspace-root-relative — `/.claude/<chid>` from the app\nside, `/<chid>` from the `.claude` side.\n\nBecause `.claude` commits once per push:\n\n- **Code-side commits** each carry one\n  `ochid: /.claude/<.claude-chid>` — the `.claude`\n  working-copy change ID, stable until `.claude` is\n  committed at push time.\n- **The `.claude` commit** carries one `ochid: /<code-chid>`\n  per code commit in that push: a single trailer when one\n  code commit is pushed (the squashed case), a list when\n  several are. The multi-line `ochid:` list and its\n  fork / multi-user generalization are designed in\n  [`forks-multi-user.md`](forks-multi-user.md).\n\nUse `vc-x1 chid -s code,bot -L` to capture the change IDs\n(first line app, second `.claude`).\n\n## Sub-cycle ladders\n\nThe local ladder of sub-cycle Work commits is typically\nkept on its own jj `@` chain and **collapsed into the\nsub-cycle's Close-out** before the parent cycle continues.\nLadder commits are scratch — they exist for review and\nbisection during the sub-cycle and disappear into one\ncommit at sub-cycle close-out.\n\n### Per-Work-commit contract within a ladder\n\nFor each Work commit in the ladder:\n\n1. `jj new -R .` — create a fresh empty `@`.\n2. Do the commit's work on this `@`.\n3. Run `cargo test --bins`. **Non-negotiable.** Build and\n   clippy alone are not enough — past experience\n   (0.41.1-6.5) had a regression introduced in an early\n   ladder commit that didn't surface until a later one ran\n   the full suite, by which point bisection cost was higher\n   than it needed to be.\n4. `jj describe -m \"...\" -m \"...\" -R .` — give it a working\n   title and body. The title does **not** need a version\n   suffix during the ladder; the sub-cycle Close-out collects\n   every commit into one final commit carrying the\n   `(X.Y.Z-N)` marker. Ladder titles are scratch — they\n   never reach the remote.\n\n### Navigating the ladder\n\nCommon moves while a ladder is active:\n\n- `jj log -r 'all()' -R .` — see the whole ladder at a\n  glance.\n- `jj edit -r <prefix> -R .` — jump `@` to any commit in\n  the ladder by chid prefix. Useful for bisection: re-run\n  the failing test at each candidate without manual\n  checkout juggling.\n- `jj edit @-- -R .` — quick-jump back two commits.\n- `jj diff -r <chid> -R .` — review one commit's diff in\n  isolation.\n\nExample using a revset with the `::` operator from the last\ncommit pushed to the remote:\n\n```\n$ jj log -r lu::\n○  pynknoqu wink@saville.com 2026-05-01 16:16:23 33612c81\n│  docs: cycle protocol pointer\n@  xqpnxtyu wink@saville.com 2026-05-01 16:16:23 f40c49a7\n│  docs: cycle protocol formal protocol\n○  lzqmlpvy wink@saville.com 2026-05-01 16:16:23 8c722f8c\n│  docs: jj-revsets.md review fixes\n○  wmmxkuoz wink@saville.com 2026-05-01 11:28:46 65a6705f\n│  chore: scratch — jj-revsets learning + scaffolding\n◆  lulqxovr wink@saville.com 2026-04-27 22:27:23 main 55eadc8e\n│  docs: capture 0.41.1 init+clone redesign plan (0.42.0-4)\n~\n```\n\nLadder work happens on whatever `@` is currently selected.\nUse `jj edit -r <chid>` to switch to whichever ladder\ncommit you want to work on. Modifications to a selected\ncommit rewrite that commit in place; descendants auto-rebase.\n\n### Close-out: squash the ladder\n\nWhen all ladder Work commits are done and `cargo test --bins`\nis green on the latest:\n\n```\njj squash --from \"<base>..@-\" --into @ -u -R .\n```\n\nWhere `<base>` is the parent of the **first** ladder\ncommit — typically the bookmark or commit the ladder\nbranched from. The `-u` flag (use destination message)\ntells jj to keep `@`'s description and discard the source\ndescriptions; the intermediate titles served their purpose,\nand `vc-x1 push` will overwrite `@`'s description anyway.\n\nAfter squash:\n\n- All ladder diffs are folded into `@`.\n- The intermediate commits become empty and are\n  abandoned automatically.\n- History is linear: `<base> → @`.\n\nThen run `vc-x1 push <bookmark>` exactly as for any other\ncommit. Push's `commit-app` stage finalizes `@` with the\nsub-cycle Close-out title/body and the proper `(X.Y.Z-N)`\nversion suffix.\n\nFor N = 1 the close-out squash is a no-op (`base..@-` is\nempty when `@-` is `<base>`); push the single commit\ndirectly.\n\n### Recovery\n\nIf a ladder commit goes wrong and you want to back out\nwithout losing prior commits:\n\n- **Discard the current commit's work.** `jj abandon @ -R .`\n  drops it and gives you a fresh empty `@` on the same\n  parent. Earlier commits are untouched.\n- **Edit an earlier commit.** `jj edit -r <chid> -R .`,\n  make corrections, then `jj edit -r <last-ladder-chid>`\n  to return. Descendants auto-rebase.\n- **Discard the entire ladder.** `jj op log -R .` shows the\n  op history; `jj op restore <op-id> -R .` reverts the repo\n  to that point. This is a full undo — it removes *all*\n  ladder work after the chosen op. Use only when you want\n  to start over. **Not** a close-out recipe; the squash\n  above is.\n\n# References\n\n- [`jj-revsets.md`](jj-revsets.md) — revset primitives\n  (chid/cid, `@`/`@-`/`@+`, `..`/`::` ranges, prefix matching).\n- [`forks-multi-user.md`](forks-multi-user.md) — multi-ochid\n  trailer design (fork / multi-user generalization).\n- [`substep-test.sh`](substep-test.sh) — script that\n  scaffolds a 4-revision ladder under `/tmp/substep-test`\n  for squash-recipe experiments.\n- 0.41.1-6.5 cycle — first multi-commit ladder usage. The\n  per-commit `cargo test --bins` gate originated there\n  after a regression introduced in an early commit wasn't\n  caught until a later one ran the full suite.\n"
}

> DEVELOPER

In preperation you it says "opening the chores section" in both ## Cycles and ## Preparation, clarify for me what is meant by "open" and what "chores section" are we referring to?

> AGENT

**"the chores section"** = the `##` section in the current `notes/chores/chores-NN.md` file that records this cycle. Each cycle gets exactly one (or rarely several — 0.55.0 had four) such section in that file. For 0.59.0, it's `## docs: cycle protocol updates (0.59.0)` at line 51 of `notes/chores/chores-12.md`.

**"open"** = write the `##` header line for this cycle into that file, with the anticipated close-out title. The section starts with just the header (and the `Commits:` line that gets backfilled in the *next* cycle's Preparation). Body content gets filled in at close-out — that's when the `## In Progress` problem statement + plan ladder move into the section per the "title only holds position" rule.

So the literal Preparation action is: append a new `## <type>: <desc> (X.Y.Z)` line to the current chores file. The section is "opened" because it now exists, but holds no narrative yet.

**Why this isn't fully spelled out in cycle-protocol.md**: the chores-file convention itself (what `notes/chores/chores-NN.md` is, the `Commits:` line, the no-edit-list rule, the commit-recording header rule) lives in CLAUDE.md's `## Pre-commit Requirements` — which we deliberately left in CLAUDE.md as a general writing convention rather than moving into the cycle protocol. So the protocol *uses* "chores section" as a term that the chores convention defines elsewhere.

If you want the protocol doc to be more self-contained on this, options:

- **Tightest**: change the `## Preparation` bullet to start with "**Open the chores section** (`notes/chores/chores-NN.md`)." Two more words, gives the reader the file path so they can find an example.
- **More**: add a cross-link to CLAUDE.md's chores convention so anyone unfamiliar can read the definition: `(see [Chores section content](../CLAUDE.md#chores-section-content--no-edit-list-git-is-the-record))`. Spends a line.
- **Most**: relocate the chores convention into cycle-protocol.md too. Broader scope, more redundancy work in `-2` and elsewhere — probably not worth it now.

> AGENT

Agree. Here's what I'd add as a new `## Chores sections` immediately after `## Cycles` (before `## Preparation`), tight enough to be a quick definition:

```
## Chores sections

A **chores section** is a `##` section in
`notes/chores/chores-NN.md` recording one cycle. The
header is the cycle's commit title (e.g. `## refactor:
foo bar (X.Y.Z)`); the body — prose intro + bullets +
optional `###` design subsections — fills in at close-out
from the `## In Progress` block. The first line under
the header is a `Commits:` line citing the recorded
commit(s) by file-local `[N]` ref, backfilled at the
*next* cycle's Preparation since the commit doesn't
exist yet when the section is written.

**"Open" the chores section** (Preparation) = append the
`##` header to the current `notes/chores/chores-NN.md`
with the anticipated close-out title. The body is empty;
content arrives at close-out (see [Close-out](#close-out)).

Fuller chores conventions — content rules ("no edit
list; git is the record"), header sync to the final
commit title, design subsection pattern, `Commits:`
formatting — live in CLAUDE.md
[Pre-commit Requirements](../CLAUDE.md#pre-commit-requirements).
```

Then the `## Preparation` bullet stays the same since "open the chores section" is now defined just above.

That's a focused fix. But it raises a related question worth flagging: the chores convention currently lives in CLAUDE.md `## Pre-commit Requirements` (specifically `### Chores section content`, `### Chores commit references`, `### Headings and entries that record a commit`). Those three subsections are about cycle bookkeeping, not general writing — they're really cycle-protocol material. If you want cycle-protocol.md to be fully self-contained for README.md to reference, those probably want to move here too.

Two paths:

- **Now (Option A — tight)**: just add the `## Chores sections` definition above; cross-link to CLAUDE.md for the fuller rules. Cheap, addresses the local-definition need, leaves the split.
- **Now or later (Option B — fuller)**: also relocate the three chores-related subsections from CLAUDE.md `## Pre-commit Requirements` into cycle-protocol.md. CLAUDE.md keeps only general writing conventions (`### Notes references`, `### Markdown anchor links`). cycle-protocol.md becomes the single source for both cycle workflow AND chores convention.

My lean: Option A now (as part of `-1`'s relocate scope, since we're already there), Option B later — capture as a `## Ideas` or `## Todo` entry once Ideas exists in `-2`. Keeps `-1` from sprawling further while putting the eventual move on the radar.

1	# Cycle protocol

3	## Cycles
4	
5	A cycle has three phases:
6	
7	- **[Preparation](#preparation)** (`X.Y.Z-0`) — the cycle's first commit.
8	  Sets up the cycle by:
9	  - Backfill the previuos cycle's chores.
10	  - Bump the version.
11	  - Adding an item to `## In Progress` typically from `## Priorities
12	    list of `## Todo`.
13	  - Succinct probem statement.
14	  - Open the chores section.
15	- **[Work-N](#work-n)** (`X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, …) — the commits
16	  that implement the change. As many as the change needs, see
17	  [per-commit flow](#per-commit-flow).
18	- **[Close-out](#close-out)** (bare `X.Y.Z`) — the cycle's last commit.
19	  - Move the current items title into `## Done`.
20	  - Move `In Progress` into a [chores section)[#chores-sections].
21	  - Optionally updates `notes/README.md`.
22	
23	A cycle's commits are published to the project remote
24	either incrementally or as one batch at close-out
25	  - The result must always be published at close-out. See
26	[Pushing](#pushing).
27

30	Preparation, `X.Y.Z-3.1` / `X.Y.Z-3.2` Work, `X.Y.Z-3`
31	Close-out. The same three-phase shape applies recursively
32	at every depth. See [Numbering](#numbering) for the
33	suffix rule and [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders)
34	for the local-ladder mechanics.

42	optional `###` design subsections — fills in at close-out
43	from the `## In Progress` block. The first line under
44	the header is a `Commits:` line citing the recorded
45	commit(s) by file-local `[N]` ref, backfilled at the
46	*next* cycle's Preparation since the commit doesn't
47	exist yet when the section is written.
48

50	`##` header to the current `notes/chores/chores-NN.md`
51	with the anticipated close-out title. The body is empty;
