54	Fuller chores conventions — content rules ("no edit
55	list; git is the record"), header sync to the final

60	## Preparation
61	
62	The cycle's first commit (`X.Y.Z-0`):
63	
64	- **Backfill the previous cycle's chores `Commits:` ref.**
65	  Grab the just-pushed commit's URL + full SHA, fill in the
66	  newest chores section that was left `Commits:`-less. See
67	  [Chores commit references](../CLAUDE.md#chores-commit-references).
68	- **Bump the version** in `Cargo.toml` to `X.Y.Z-0`.
69	- **Pick up a `## Todo` item** (if the cycle has one). Move
70	  that entry's text out of `## Todo` and into `## In
71	  Progress` — a **bold title line** (exact match for the
72	  chores section header, minus the `## ` prefix) +
73	  **succinct problem statement** + **plan ladder**. See
74	  [Prose form](../CLAUDE.md#prose-form) (Problem + plan shape).
75	- **Open the chores section.** Header = the cycle's
76	  **anticipated close-out title** (e.g. `## refactor: foo
77	  bar (X.Y.Z)`). No body — the cycle's live narrative
78	  lives in `## In Progress` and moves into the section at
79	  close-out per the "title only holds position" rule. One
80	  chores section per cycle is the default; per-commit
81	  sections (like 0.55.0's four) are a deliberate choice.
82

84	
85	The cycle's work commits implement the change. Number as

88	Interim pushes are optional (backup, progress visibility);

> DEVELOPER

Review my changes to the three phases in ## Cycles, I've tried to match the information in corresponding sub-sections.

> AGENT

Reviewed. Most of the substance is right — there are some concrete defects and one design-level question.

**Concrete defects to fix:**

1. Line 9: typo `previuos` → `previous`.
2. Line 11-12: `## Priorities` missing closing backtick; the line reads `'## Priorities\n    list of '## Todo'` with broken backticks — likely meant `from `## Priorities` (references into `## Todo`)`.
3. Line 13: typo `probem` → `problem`.
4. Line 20: broken markdown link `[chores section)[#chores-sections]` — uses `)` and `[` instead of `]` and `(`. Should be `[chores section](#chores-sections)`.
5. Line 19: `Move the current items title` — possessive `item's`, and "current item" is vague; the `## Close-out` subsection calls it "the picked-up item." Either name match.
6. Line 20: ``Move `In Progress` into a [chores section]`` — missing `##` prefix and code formatting (`` `## In Progress` ``). Also "a chores section" implies any; it's *the* one opened during Preparation.
7. Lines 24-26: indentation glitch — the "The result must always be published at close-out" line is a 2-space-indented `-` bullet hanging off a non-list paragraph. Either inline it (`incrementally or as one batch at close-out; the result must always be published at close-out`) or restructure as a real list.

**Dropped info — intentional or oversight?**

- Preparation bullet sub-list mentions `## In Progress` and "succinct problem statement" but drops the **plan ladder** (the third element of the In Progress entry per the subsection). If aiming for overview parity, that's missing.
- Close-out's `notes/README.md` bullet drops the `if functionality changed (new flags, new subcommands, changed behavior)` qualifier — without it, "Optionally update `notes/README.md`" reads as if you might update it for any reason.

**Design-level observation:**

The detail level is now uneven across the three phases:

- **Preparation**: 5 sub-bullets (close to subsection parity)
- **Work-N**: 0 sub-bullets (one inline line)
- **Close-out**: 3 sub-bullets (close to subsection parity)

Two consistent shapes are reasonable:

- **(i) Terse overview** — single-line description per phase (like current Work-N); subsection holds operational detail. Tighter; subsection is the only place rules are stated; no drift risk.
- **(ii) Mini-summary** — sub-bullets recap subsection actions for at-a-glance scan (current Preparation/Close-out shape). More scannable; duplicates subsection content — needs both kept in sync when rules change.

You've picked (ii) for Preparation/Close-out and (i) for Work-N. Either shape works, but probably want to commit to one for all three. (i) is the cheaper option long-term; (ii) is better for a reader who wants to skim without scrolling.

Want me to apply the defect fixes, or do you want to make them yourself? On the design question — pick (i) or (ii) or leave mixed?

> DEVELOPER

go with (i), apply the fixes

Reviewed `## Preparation` changes. The new versions are tighter, but a few defects + design observations:

**Defects in the new versions:**

1. **Pick up Todo bullet** (lines 71-77):
   - Line 73: missing period after `Progress`.
   - Line 75: stray closing `)` with no opening paren — `"minus the `## ` prefix)"`.
   - Line 76 starts mid-sentence with `**succinct problem statement** + **plan ladder**.` — reads as a fragment. Probably wants the three In Progress elements as three parallel sub-bullets:
     ```
     - **Pick up a `## Todo` item** (if the cycle has one).
       Move into `## In Progress` as:
       - A **bold title line** — exact match of the chores
         section header, minus the `## ` prefix.
       - A **succinct problem statement**.
       - A **plan ladder**.

       See [Prose form](../CLAUDE.md#prose-form).
     ```

2. **Open chores section bullet** (lines 86-91):
   - Missing periods after `close-out` (line 90) and `default` (line 91).

3. **Backfill bullet** (line 61): "previous cycle's chores" — drops "section" or "`Commits:` ref"; reads slightly vague. Suggest `"previous cycle's chores section `Commits:` ref"` for term consistency with `## Chores sections`.

**Design observation worth flagging:**

The new **Open the chores section** bullet (lines 86-91) restates content that's already in `## Chores sections` (header is the anticipated close-out title, body empty, etc.). Now that we have a dedicated definition section, the Preparation bullet could collapse to a pointer:

```
- **Open the [chores section](#chores-sections)** — append a
  `##` header with the cycle's anticipated close-out title.
```

That's one line. The rule details (no body, content arrives at close-out, one per cycle default) live in `## Chores sections` and don't get re-stated here. Saves duplication.

**Also (carry-over from `## Chores sections`):** the parenthetical you added is misplaced — currently reads `live in CLAUDE.md (content rules, ...) [Pre-commit Requirements]`, which puts the parenthetical between the noun and the link awkwardly. Cleaner:

```
Fuller chores conventions (content rules, header sync,
design subsection pattern, `Commits:` formatting) live
in CLAUDE.md
[Pre-commit Requirements](../CLAUDE.md#pre-commit-requirements).
```

Also `Commits:` should keep its backticks (it's the literal field name).

## Chores sections

A **chores section** is a `##` section in
`notes/chores/chores-NN.md` recording one cycle.

The phrase **"Open" the chores section** means append a
`##` header to the current `notes/chores/chores-NN.md`
with the cycle's anticipated close-out title (e.g.
`## refactor: foo bar (X.Y.Z)`). The body is empty;
content arrives at close-out (see [Close-out](#close-out)).

Fuller chores conventions (content rules, header sync,
design subsection pattern, `Commits:` formatting) live
in CLAUDE.md
[Pre-commit Requirements](../CLAUDE.md#pre-commit-requirements).

## Preparation

The cycle's first commit (`X.Y.Z-0`):

- **Backfill the previous cycle's chores section
  `Commits:` ref** — see
  [Chores commit references](../CLAUDE.md#chores-commit-references).
- **Bump the version** in `Cargo.toml` to `X.Y.Z-0`.

  Move into `## In Progress` as:
  - A **bold title line** — exact match of the chores
    section header, minus the `## ` prefix.
  - A **succinct problem statement**.
  - A **plan ladder**.

  See [Prose form](../CLAUDE.md#prose-form).
- **Open the [chores section](#chores-sections)** —
  append a `##` header with the cycle's anticipated
  close-out title.

> AGENT

Done. Work-N now matches the bulleted shape of Preparation and Close-out:

- Short intro line ending in `:`
- 3 bullets covering per-commit flow, interim push policy, sub-cycle subdivision

Bold emphasis is mixed-style on purpose: bullet 1 emphasizes the link target (per-commit flow is the key mechanism), bullet 2 leads with the topic (interim pushes — it's a policy not an action), bullet 3 leads with the action (subdivide). The Preparation/Close-out bullets do the same — `**Backfill ...**` and `**Bump ...**` are actions, while `**Open the [chores section]**` puts emphasis on the topic. ## Close-out

The cycle's last commit (bare `X.Y.Z`) does bookkeeping
only — the commit body describes that bookkeeping, not
what happens post-squash:

- **Move the picked-up item** from `## In Progress` to a
  one-line entry (with a chores `[N]` ref) in `## Done`.
- **Move the `## In Progress` block into the
  [chores section](#chores-sections)** (the title-only
  placeholder opened during Preparation):
  - Cut the problem statement + plan ladder out of
    `## In Progress` (drop the bold title line — the
    chores `##` header already carries that string).

  - Sync the chores header to the **final** commit title
    if the cycle's scope shifted; update every anchor
    back-reference.
  - Add an `### As-built ladder` listing the cycle's
    commits; optional `### Outcome` notes.
  - Replace `## In Progress` with
    `_No cycle currently in progress._`.
- **Update `notes/README.md`** if functionality changed
  (new flags, new subcommands, changed behavior).

Whether to **squash** the cycle into one commit before the
publishing push, or push as-is, is decided at push time —
see [Pushing](#pushing).

separator — `.` — at every depth (semver mandates `-` once at

> AGENT

Here's what I'd do for `## Numbering`:

**Changes:**

1. Drop the semver parenthetical ("semver mandates `-` once at the start..." — rationale, not rule; the examples make the format unambiguous).
2. Promote "**trailing `0` identifier marks a Preparation**" into the intro line — it's a universal rule that applies at every nesting depth, not just within Nesting.
3. Restructure the dense Nesting paragraph into a parallel bullet list matching the basic-suffix bullets — shows the symmetry and is scannable.
4. Keep the "no Preparation" carve-out as a tight one-line paragraph after the Nesting bullets.
5. Tighten the Cargo.toml paragraph (drop the "Each commit carries its phase version in its title **and**" preamble — the bullets above already establish that titles carry the suffix).

**Proposed result:**

```
## Numbering

The version suffix on each commit encodes its phase —
a **trailing `0` identifier marks a Preparation**:

- `X.Y.Z-0` — Preparation
- `X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, … — Work commits
- `X.Y.Z` — Close-out (bare version, no suffix)

**Nesting.** Sub-cycles append another level, recursively:

- `X.Y.Z-3.0` — Preparation of the `-3` sub-cycle
- `X.Y.Z-3.1`, `X.Y.Z-3.2` — its Work
- `X.Y.Z-3` — its Close-out
- `X.Y.Z-3.1.0` — Preparation of the `-3.1` sub-sub-cycle

Sub-cycles needing no Preparation omit `.0` (`-3.1`,
`-3.2`, `-3`); one that grows a Preparation later adds
`-3.0` without renumbering siblings.

Bump `Cargo.toml` at the start of each phase so `vc-x1 -V`
reports the active phase at build time.
```

That's 19 lines (current is 22). Apply, or want changes?

> AGENT

Honest assessment — this is genuinely useful to step back on.

**Yes, we're losing some information.** Most of it is rationale/history (Why numeric suffixes, Ordering caveat, 0.41.1-6.7 anchors, validation dates, worked examples). That's the cheap stuff to lose — it doesn't change what the rules are, only why they exist. I can rederive the rationale if needed; the rules themselves stayed.

**But a few losses do hit operational substance** that I'd want back:

1. **"Pick up a `## Todo` item that duplicates current `## In Progress` work is a process bug"** — this was a defensive check that catches a real failure mode (picking up a Todo I'm already working on). Cheap one-liner; I'd restore it.
2. **"per-commit sections (like 0.55.0's four) are a deliberate choice"** — explicit permission for the per-commit chores carve-out. Without it, I'd default to "one per cycle is mandatory" instead of "default with documented alternative." Cheap to restore — one line in `## Chores sections`.
3. **`## Pushing > After finalize` second paragraph** — the "even when the next step seems obvious. Wait." emphasis was load-bearing *specifically* because of the temptation to auto-proceed. Consolidating to one paragraph weakened the emphasis on a rule I have to follow.
4. **`## Sub-cycle ladders > Worked example`** — a judgment call. Removed 40 lines; cost is that the first time I'm asked to drive a sub-cycle ladder, I have rules but no concrete walkthrough. A 5-10 line mini-example showing `jj new` × N → `jj squash --from "<base>..@-"` → push would preserve most of the value at 1/4 the cost.

**Ambiguity introduced:**

- "**trailing `0` identifier marks a Preparation**" — easy to misread as "ends in 0." Should be "the final identifier is `0`" or similar to disambiguate `-10` (Work) vs `-1.0` (Preparation). Worth a one-word fix.
- "Sub-cycles needing no Preparation omit `.0`" — but *when* does a sub-cycle not need Preparation? Not stated. A reader has to infer from context.
- `## Cycles` overview duplicates content with the dedicated sections. Real drift risk over time: when rules change in `## Preparation`, the overview sub-bullets need parallel updates or they go stale.

**Readability for you:** clear net win. Bullets scan faster than walls of prose; the file is now skimmable.

**Operational fitness for me:** net win, with the 3-4 restorations above. Without them, mostly net win but with a few specific failure modes more likely (Todo duplication, per-commit chores edge case, after-finalize auto-proceed temptation).

**Recommendation:** I'd restore items 1-3 (low-cost, real value) and write a tight worked example for item 4 (40 lines → 8 lines). Plus the "final identifier" disambiguation. Probably 15-20 lines of net additions to a now-538-line file. Want me to draft those restorations for review?

**4. Worked example** (`## Sub-cycle ladders`, new subsection after `### Recovery`):

```
### Worked example

A 3-commit ladder from `main`, then close-out squash:

```
jj new -R .                   # repeat per commit:
... edit files; cargo test --bins ...
jj describe -m "..." -R .

jj squash --from "main..@-" --into @ -u -R .
```

Result: linear `main → @` with all ladder diffs folded
into `@`; intermediate commits abandoned. Then
`vc-x1 push main …` per normal.
```

**5. Numbering disambiguation** (`## Numbering`, intro line):

> The version suffix on each commit encodes its phase —
> the **final identifier `0` marks a Preparation**:

Change: `trailing `0` identifier` → `final identifier `0``. Now `-10` (Work #10) and `-1.0` (Preparation of `-1` sub-cycle) are unambiguous.

---

Total addition: ~14 lines net (1 + 1 + 1 + 11 for the example + 0-word fix in Numbering). Apply all five?

> DEVELOPER

any issues with creating a new node then squashing after I review?

> AGENT

No issues — that's exactly the sub-cycle ladder pattern we just documented. Both commits will be undescribed (the current `@` is your `-1` working copy, the new `@` will be the restoration draft), and `jj squash` defaults to squashing `@` into `@-`, which is what you want.

Three small notes:

1. **Diff review**: after I make the restoration edits on the new `@`, you can review just those changes with `jj diff` (working-copy diff vs. `@-`) — clean isolation from the other `-1` edits.
2. **No description churn**: neither commit has a description set, so squash won't ask which to keep. Result: `@-` accumulates the restoration diffs into the broader `-1` working copy; new empty `@` lands on top.
3. **Rollback if needed**: if you reject the restorations, `jj abandon @` drops the draft cleanly; the original `-1` working copy is untouched.

Want me to run `jj new -R .`, apply all five restorations there, and report back so you can `jj diff` them?

505	empty when `@-` is `<base>`); push the single commit
506	directly.
507	
508	### Recovery
509	
510	If a ladder commit goes wrong and you want to back out
511	without losing prior commits:
512	
513	- **Discard the current commit's work.** `jj abandon @ -R .`
514	  drops it and gives you a fresh empty `@` on the same
515	  parent. Earlier commits are untouched.
516	- **Edit an earlier commit.** `jj edit -r <chid> -R .`,
517	  make corrections, then `jj edit -r <last-ladder-chid>`
518	  to return. Descendants auto-rebase.
519	- **Discard the entire ladder.** `jj op log -R .` shows the
520	  op history; `jj op restore <op-id> -R .` reverts the repo
521	  to that point. This is a full undo — it removes *all*
522	  ladder work after the chosen op. Use only when you want
523	  to start over. **Not** a close-out recipe; the squash
524	  above is.
525	
526	# References
527	
528	- [`jj-revsets.md`](jj-revsets.md) — revset primitives
529	  (chid/cid, `@`/`@-`/`@+`, `..`/`::` ranges, prefix matching).
530	- [`forks-multi-user.md`](forks-multi-user.md) — multi-ochid
531	  trailer design (fork / multi-user generalization).
532	- [`substep-test.sh`](substep-test.sh) — script that
533	  scaffolds a 4-revision ladder under `/tmp/substep-test`
534	  for squash-recipe experiments.
```

Use only when you want\n  to start over. **Not** a close-out recipe; the squash\n  above is.\n\n### Worked example\n\nA 3-commit ladder from `main`, then close-out squash:\n\n```\njj new -R .                   # repeat per commit:\n... edit files; cargo test --bins ...\njj describe -m \"...\" -R .\n\njj squash --from \"main..@-\" --into @ -u -R .\n```\n\nResult: linear `main → @` with all ladder diffs folded\ninto `@`; intermediate commits abandoned. Then\n`vc-x1 push main …` per normal.\n\n# References"
}

> AGENT

`## Per-commit flow` is the cycle's most-referenced section, and step 6 is a self-contained format spec buried inside a numbered list. My instinct is to **extract** rather than just tighten in place.

**Proposal: extract step 6 into a new `## Commit description` section** placed immediately after `## Per-commit flow`. Step 6 becomes:

```
6. **Write the commit description** — see
   [Commit description](#commit-description).
```

The new section absorbs the format spec, restructured with `###` subheadings (matching the `## Sub-cycle ladders` precedent):

```
## Commit description

[Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/)
with a version suffix:

```
<type>: <short description> (<version>)
```

### Title

- ≤50 chars total (version suffix counts).
- Common types: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `test`,
  `docs`, `chore`.
- Favor terse phrasings.

### Body

[Prose form](../CLAUDE.md#prose-form) (intro + bullets),
wrap ≤72. Bullet content differs per repo:

- **App-repo body**: file-by-file. One bullet per file
  changed (file plus a one-line gist). Sub-bullets for
  files with multiple distinct changes:

  ```
  - path/to/file1
    - first distinct change
    - second distinct change, wrapping to next line
       with continuation indented 5
  ```

  The file-by-file list is the source of truth for the
  cycle's mechanical change record. Chores carries the
  narrative + design, not a copy of it. Promote any
  "why" beyond one sentence to a chores `###`
  subsection.

- **Session-repo (`.claude`) body**: bullets describe
  in-session activity rather than code changes.

### Trailer

`ochid:` as the last line of the body — see
[ochid trailers](#ochid-trailers).
```

**Other tightening in `## Per-commit flow`:**

- Step 8's shell example (the `jj commit -m ... -R .` template) stays — it's a useful "how to invoke" reference. But could move to `## Commit description` as the assembled example if you prefer the per-commit-flow numbered list to be purely declarative.

**Tradeoff:**

- Per-commit-flow becomes a clean 8-step process, uniformly scannable.
- `## Commit description` is independently navigable (README.md or chores can link to it).
- Net file size ~unchanged (step 6 shrinks from ~25 lines to 2; new section is ~30 lines).
- Cost: one more section heading; reader following per-commit-flow has to follow a link to see the format.

Want extraction (with or without moving step 8's shell example)? Or prefer in-place tightening of step 6?

153	
154	Bump `Cargo.toml` at the start of each phase so `vc-x1 -V`
155	reports the active phase at build time.
156	
157	## Per-commit flow
158	
159	Every commit (Preparation, each Work commit, Close-out) goes
160	through:
161	
162	1. **Mark this commit `(current)`** as the first edit in
163	   `notes/todo.md > ## In Progress`, before any code/doc
164	   work.
165	2. **Do the work.**
166	3. **Flip this commit `(current)` → `(done)`** in `## In
167	   Progress` — before the cargo cycle and the commit, so
168	   the commit captures the completed state.
169	4. **Cargo cycle** (skip-able for purely-docs commits; full
170	   cycle mandatory at close-out):
171	   1. `cargo fmt`
172	   2. `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`
173	   3. `cargo test`
174	   4. `cargo install --path . --locked`
175	   5. (re-test if anything substantive changed)
176	5. **Work review.** Stop *before* writing any description;
177	   tell the user "ready to commit." The user reviews the
178	   working-copy changes in their editor. Most change
179	   requests come here — iterate until the work is right.
180	6. **Write the commit description.** [Conventional
181	   Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/) with a
182	   version suffix:
183	
184	   ```
185	   <type>: <short description> (<version>)
186	   ```
187	
188	   - **Title**: ≤50 chars. Common types: `feat`, `fix`,
189	     `refactor`, `test`, `docs`, `chore`. The version
190	     suffix counts toward the 50 — favor terse phrasings.
191	   - **Body**: [Prose form](../CLAUDE.md#prose-form) (intro
192	     + bullets), wrap ≤72. App-repo bullets are
193	     **file-by-file** — one bullet per file changed, file
194	     plus a one-line gist. When a file has several distinct
195	     changes, list them as `-` sub-bullets under the file
196	     bullet.
197	
198	     ```
199	     - path/to/file1
200	       - first distinct change
201	       - second distinct change, wrapping to next line
202	          with continuation indented 5
203	     ```
204	
205	     The file-by-file list is the source of truth for the
206	     cycle's mechanical change record; chores carries the
207	     narrative + design, not a copy of it. Promote any
208	     "why" beyond one sentence to a chores `###`
209	     subsection.
210	   - **`ochid:` trailer** as the last line of the body —
211	     see [ochid trailers](#ochid-trailers).
212	   - **Session-repo (`.claude`) body**: same shape; bullets
213	     describe in-session activity rather than code changes.
214	7. **Commit Description review.** Show the title + body
215	   and stop. The user reviews the description. Iterate.
216	8. **Commit.** `jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R .` for
217	   the app repo, `-R .claude` for the bot repo (`-R` last
218	   keeps the verb visible):
219	
220	   ```
221	   jj commit -m \
222	   "<type>: <short description> (<version>)" \
223	   -m "<intro paragraph>
224	
225	   - file1: gist
226	   - file2: gist
227	
228	   ochid: /.claude/<chid>" \
229	   -R .
230	   ```

> AGENT

Looking at `## Reviewing changes` (~26 lines). It's a mix of workflow note + jj reference + Zed-specific editor config.

**Proposed tightening:**

- **Drop the Zed editor config block** (~5 lines: the `~/.config/jj/config.toml` `diff-editor` setup). That's one-time user setup, not cycle protocol. Belongs in a developer-setup doc if anywhere.
- **Drop the "Pipe through `delta` or `diff-so-fancy`" tip** — tooling preference, not protocol.
- **Drop "Zed shows the diff naturally; for terminal use:"** — Zed-specific framing.
- **Convert the jj commands from a code block to bullets** — matches the file's prevailing format; each command + description on its own line.
- **Keep the `jj edit -r @-` warning** — real footgun, operationally important.
- **Keep the cross-refs paragraph** at the end.

**Proposed result:**

```
## Reviewing changes

Work review looks at the **uncommitted working-copy diff**,
on the way to commit. Common commands:

- `jj diff` — working-copy diff (uncommitted)
- `jj diff -r @-` — diff of the previous commit
- `jj diff --from <X> --to <Y>` — any two revisions
- `jj show -r <X>` — description + diff for one rev

Don't `jj edit -r @-` to view a past commit — that marks
it mutable and shifts `@`; use `jj diff -r @-` or
`jj show -r @-`.

See [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders) for the
close-out squash recipe and recovery; revset primitives
are in [`jj-revsets.md`](jj-revsets.md).
```

That's ~15 lines (was ~26).

**Tradeoff to flag:** dropping the Zed/delta tips means a new contributor setting up jj won't find that guidance here. My take: that belongs in a setup doc (or jj's own docs), not in the cycle protocol — protocol describes what to do, not how to make your tools pretty.

Apply on a new node? Or do you want to keep more (e.g., the `delta` tip)?

```
268	## Pushing
269	
270	**Push is discretionary** — done when you want to back up
271	work or publish interim progress, and **always at
272	close-out** (the cycle's result must be published). Interim
273	pushes are a judgment call; nothing forces push-per-commit.
274	
275	**Post close-out review (close-out push only).** After
276	the close-out commit and before any `jj squash` / `jj
277	rebase` / `jj git push` invocation, stop and ask the user
278	which shape the cycle lands as (squash / merge / keep)
279	and to approve the push. The choice changes the commits
280	on the target and is hard to undo cleanly once pushed;
281	treat it like the Work and Commit Description reviews —
282	surface the options, wait for the user, don't auto-pick.
283	ESC-ESC and the deviation override apply as usual.
284	
285	**Squash, merge, or keep at the close-out push.** Before
286	the publishing push, decide what shape the cycle lands as:
287	
288	- **Squash to one commit** — single entry on the target;
289	  per-commit granularity preserved only in the commit
290	  body's edit list. Right when the work is one logical
291	  change with intermediate validation points. Cost: the
292	  per-commit chores sections collapse into one (its header
293	  becomes the close-out commit title), and every anchor
294	  back-ref to a now-gone section gets re-pointed — real
295	  work, done as part of the squash.
296	- **Merge (non-FF)** — `main` gains one merge commit
297	  (`X.Y.Z`) with parents `<prev>` and the cycle tip; the
298	  sub-step commits stay reachable as the merge's second
299	  parent. `git log` shows everything; `git log
300	  --first-parent` shows main as one jump per cycle. Best
301	  when the sub-steps are worth preserving but main should
302	  read linearly. The paired `.claude` commit's body
303	  carries a multi-line ochid listing every app commit in
304	  the push — the multi-ochid case
305	  [`forks-multi-user.md`](forks-multi-user.md) designs.
306	  Set up with `jj rebase -r <tip> -d <prev> -d <sub-tip>`
307	  before invoking `jj git push`.
308	- **Keep separate** — one commit per cycle entry on main —
309	  when the decomposition is itself informative and you
310	  want `git log` to show every step. Each chores section
311	  keeps its own header / `Commits:` ref; no consolidation
312	  churn.
313	
314	If squashing or merging, do it before invoking `vc-x1
315	push` (or push manually with `jj git push` for
316	non-standard shapes).
317	
318	The flow is wrapped by `vc-x1 push <bookmark>`, which runs:
319	
320	1. Preflight (`vc-x1 sync --check` + `cargo fmt` / `clippy`
321	   / `test`).
322	2. **Work-review prompt** — shows `jj diff --stat` for
323	   both repos and asks `[y/N]`.
324	3. **Commit-description-review prompt** — uses `--title`
325	   / `--body` or opens `$EDITOR`.
326	4. Commits both repos with ochid trailers, advances the
327	   bookmark, `jj git push --bookmark <bookmark>` for the
328	   app, finalizes `.claude` (its single per-push commit).
329	
330	`<bookmark>` is the **app-side working branch** (often
331	`main`, sometimes a feature branch). `.claude` always pushes
332	its `main` regardless of the app-side bookmark. See
333	`vc-x1 push --help` for the full flag list, resumability
334	(`--from`, `--restart`, `--status`), and recovery; CLAUDE.md
335	keeps only the rules that govern bot behavior. `vc-x1 sync
336	--check` may also be run manually before editing to surface
337	remote changes early.
338	
339	**`.claude` cadence — once per push.** The `.claude`
340	working-copy node accumulates session data across the
341	cycle's commits and is described + committed when a push
342	happens — one push, one `.claude` commit, paired with every
343	code commit in that push. Its change ID is stable from the
344	first code commit that references it through to that
345	`.claude` commit (working-copy snapshots, `jj describe`, and
346	the finalize `jj commit` all preserve the change ID), so the
347	`ochid:` trailers resolve across the push.
348	
349	`.claude` is a linear journal — all session work lives on
350	`main`, regardless of the app-side bookmark. **Do not
351	create or maintain `.claude` bookmarks that mirror app-side
352	branches** — risks the bot steering session pushes to the
353	wrong remote ref.
354	
355	**Bot communication at the reviews.** Use plain prose —
356	no insider jargon ("Gate N signal", "Checkpoint N", etc.):
357	
358	- **At Work review** — summarize what changed and stop.
359	  "Work complete. Please review."
360	- **At Commit Description review** — present `$TITLE` and
361	  `$BODY` explicitly, then ask permission to commit (or
362	  push). Don't spell out the full `vc-x1 push <bookmark>
363	  --yes --title "$TITLE" --body "$BODY"` invocation by
364	  default; it's mechanical and obvious from title/body.
365	  Show it only on explicit request.
366	- **At Post close-out review** — surface the shape
367	  options (squash / merge / keep) with a brief trade-off
368	  summary, plus the push target; wait for the user's
369	  choice before any `jj squash` / `jj rebase` / `jj git
370	  push` invocation.
371	
372	**After finalize: stop and wait.** After `vc-x1 finalize`
373	launches — whether mid-session per-push or at session-end —
374	you **MUST NEVER** proceed to a next step, edit files, run
375	tools, or emit any text until the user explicitly directs
376	you to continue. **This holds even when the next step
377	seems obvious — wait.** Treat finalize as a hard stop for
378	the whole turn; any final words go in the approval prompt
379	*before* executing finalize. The user controls cadence —
380	auto-proceeding bypasses the push+finalize checkpoint.
381	
382	If push exits before `finalize-claude` (e.g. failure between
383	`push-app` and `finalize-claude`), run finalize by hand:
384	
385	```
386	vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
387	```
388	
389	Clear push's saved state after any out-of-band recovery —
390	`rm .vc-x1/push-state.toml` or `vc-x1 push <bookmark>
391	--restart` — otherwise push resumes from a stale stage.
392	
393	A late code-repo tweak after `push-app` succeeded (e.g.
394	updating CLAUDE.md or memory) requires `jj squash
395	--ignore-immutable` and a re-push; that is a remote rewrite
396	and needs explicit approval like any push.
397	
398	## ochid trailers
399	
```

> AGENT

`## Pushing` is the file's biggest section (~130 lines) and a mix of three things: protocol rules, `vc-x1 push` tool reference, and recovery recipes. Mostly wall-of-prose with bold leads acting as informal subsection headers.

**Main proposal: subdivide with `###` headings + tighten prose.**

Subsections:

- `### Policy` — discretionary during cycle, mandatory at close-out (3 lines).
- `### Shape at close-out push` — the squash / merge / keep decision + post-close-out review + the "if squashing/merging, do it before `vc-x1 push`" note (~25 lines, cleaned up).
- `### vc-x1 push wrapper` — the 4-step internals + `<bookmark>` semantics + pointer to `--help` (~12 lines).
- `### .claude cadence` — once-per-push + linear-journal + bookmark admonition (~12 lines).
- `### Bot communication at the reviews` — the three review-style notes (~12 lines).
- `### After finalize: stop and wait` — the hard-stop rule (~8 lines).
- `### Recovery` — finalize-by-hand recipe + clear saved state + late-tweak rule (~12 lines).

**Tightening within each:**

- The "Post close-out review" bold block folds into `### Shape at close-out push` as an opening paragraph (it's about how to handle the shape decision, so it belongs there).
- The Merge bullet has some verbose prose ("Best when... read linearly. The paired `.claude` commit's body carries a multi-line ochid...") — the multi-ochid pointer to `forks-multi-user.md` is useful but the rest can compress.
- The Cargo.toml + `vc-x1 push --help` references stay; jj/CLAUDE history references trim.

Expected end size: ~110 lines (current 130). Subdivision is the bigger win — the section becomes navigable instead of one long scroll.

**Alternative I considered and rejected for this pass:** extract the `vc-x1 push` tool details (4-step internals, recovery recipes) into a separate `## vc-x1 push` section or a sibling `notes/vc-x1-push.md`. That's ~30 lines of tool-reference content. Reason against: the tool implements the protocol, and co-location makes both more readable. If you'd like the split anyway, it's a clean follow-up.

Apply the subdivision + tightening on the current `@` (`kmqrmtmr` is already empty post-squash, ready to take the edits)?

> DEVELOPER

LG squash, but one Q this incremental change technique, loop(change + squash), for a major refactor is working well. Thoughts on where we should capture it, maybe "## Per-commit flow" as a sub-range in "2. **Do the work.**" or have "**Do the work.**" reference a "## Do the work" section?

> AGENT

Good question — yes, this loop technique deserves a documented home. My lean is **a new tight `## Iterative work` section**, placed right before `## Sub-cycle ladders` (so the two `jj @`-manipulation topics group together), with a tiny cross-link from step 2 of `## Per-commit flow` so it's discoverable from the flow.

**Why a separate section rather than inline in step 2:**

- Step 2 currently reads as one line ("Do the work."). Inlining the loop instructions would make step 2 disproportionately long for what's conceptually one step of the flow.
- The technique is reusable — chores-section design iteration, doc rewrites, code refactors — not specific to a phase. A standalone section is more discoverable for any of those.
- Sub-cycle ladders uses the same jj primitives (`jj new` + `jj squash`) but for a different scope (multi-commit). Cross-linking the two makes the relationship explicit.

**Why not fold into `## Sub-cycle ladders` as a `### Single-commit iteration` subsection:**

- Conceptually distinct scope: sub-cycles have their own `X.Y.Z-N.M` numbering; in-commit iteration doesn't change the version suffix. Mixing them in one section risks confusing the two.
- Easier to find as `## Iterative work` than nested under Sub-cycle ladders.

**Proposed content (~13 lines):**

```
## Iterative work

When work for a single commit benefits from incremental
review, loop:

1. `jj new -R .` — fresh empty `@` on top of the target
   commit.
2. Make the next round of changes.
3. Review with `jj diff -R .` (just this round).
4. `jj squash -R .` to fold into the target; jj
   auto-creates a new empty `@` for the next round.

Same jj mechanics as a
[sub-cycle ladder](#sub-cycle-ladders), but at
single-commit scope — the cycle's version suffix
doesn't change.
```

And the step 2 tweak:

```
2. **Do the work** (see [Iterative work](#iterative-work)
   for the loop-and-squash technique).
```

**Plus this cycle should squash the current `## Pushing` restructure in first** — it's still on the working node. Then a fresh node for the `## Iterative work` addition.

Approve the design + placement? If yes, I'll squash Pushing, then jj new for Iterative work.

> DEVELOPER

LG, but I use zed and/or vscode (vscode has a better markdown renderer, handles references well) and not jj, but that info is good just pointing it out and we might mention it.

> AGENT

Good catch — your editor-based review flow is the reality; the `jj diff` commands are bot/terminal fallback. I'll mention it in `## Reviewing changes` and frame the new `## Iterative work` step 3 to defer there. Squashing Pushing first, then making the additions on a new node.

> AGENT

`## ochid trailers` is already pretty tight (22 lines) — the wins here are modest. Two small things I'd touch:

1. **Merge the path definition into the intro line** — currently two sentences; can be one.
2. **Reframe "Because `.claude` commits once per push:" → "Asymmetric because `.claude` commits once per push:"** — makes the *purpose* of the lead-in (explaining why the two bullets are different shapes) explicit.
3. **Tighten the `.claude` bullet's trailing multi-ochid sentence** — same content, fewer words.

**Proposed result (~16 lines, was 22):**

```
## ochid trailers

Each commit body carries `ochid:` trailer(s) —
workspace-root-relative paths linking it to its
counterpart(s) in the other repo. Asymmetric because
`.claude` commits once per push:

- **Code-side commits** each carry one
  `ochid: /.claude/<.claude-chid>` — the `.claude`
  working-copy change ID, stable until `.claude` is
  committed at push time.
- **The `.claude` commit** carries one
  `ochid: /<code-chid>` per code commit in that push: a
  single trailer for one code commit (the squashed
  case), or a multi-line list for several (see
  [`forks-multi-user.md`](forks-multi-user.md)).

Use `vc-x1 chid -s code,bot -L` to capture the change
IDs (first line app, second `.claude`).
```

**Note**: this section doesn't yet codify the **ochid invariant** ("every public ochid resolves in the public graph") or the bot-side rules (never squash, descriptions editable) — those are scoped to `-2` per the current plan. So this pass keeps `## ochid trailers` describing only the existing trailer mechanics; the invariant rules land in `-2` along with the cross-repo / God-repo design.

```
270	## Pushing
271	
272	### Policy
273	
274	Push is **discretionary** during the cycle (backup,
275	progress visibility) and **mandatory at close-out** —
276	the cycle's result must be published.
277	
278	### Shape at close-out push
279	
280	Before the publishing push, decide the shape and get
281	user approval. The choice changes commits on the target
282	and is hard to undo; treat like Work / Commit
283	Description reviews — surface the options, wait for the
284	user, don't auto-pick.
285	
286	- **Squash to one commit** — single entry on the target;
287	  per-commit granularity preserved only in the commit
288	  body's edit list. Right when the work is one logical
289	  change with intermediate validation points. Cost: the
290	  per-commit chores sections collapse into one (its
291	  header becomes the close-out commit title); every
292	  anchor back-ref to a now-gone section gets re-pointed.
293	- **Merge (non-FF)** — `main` gains one merge commit
294	  (`X.Y.Z`) with parents `<prev>` and the cycle tip; the
295	  cycle commits stay reachable as the merge's second
296	  parent. `git log --first-parent` shows main as one jump
297	  per cycle. Set up with `jj rebase -r <tip> -d <prev>
298	  -d <sub-tip>` before pushing. The paired `.claude`
299	  commit carries a multi-line ochid (see
300	  [ochid trailers](#ochid-trailers)).
301	- **Keep separate** — one commit per cycle entry on
302	  `main`. Use when the decomposition itself is
303	  informative. Each chores section keeps its own header /
304	  `Commits:` ref; no consolidation churn.
305	
306	If squashing or merging, do it before invoking
307	`vc-x1 push` (or push manually with `jj git push` for
308	non-standard shapes).
309	
310	### vc-x1 push wrapper
311	
312	`vc-x1 push <bookmark>` wraps:
313	
314	1. Preflight (`vc-x1 sync --check` + `cargo fmt` /
315	   `clippy` / `test`).
316	2. **Work-review prompt** — shows `jj diff --stat` for
317	   both repos and asks `[y/N]`.
318	3. **Commit-description-review prompt** — uses `--title`
319	   / `--body` or opens `$EDITOR`.
320	4. Commits both repos with ochid trailers, advances the
321	   bookmark, `jj git push --bookmark <bookmark>` for the
322	   app, finalizes `.claude` (its single per-push commit).
323	
324	`<bookmark>` is the app-side working branch (often
325	`main`, sometimes a feature branch); `.claude` always
326	pushes its `main` regardless. See `vc-x1 push --help`
327	for the full flag list, resumability (`--from`,
328	`--restart`, `--status`), and recovery. Run `vc-x1 sync
329	--check` manually before editing to surface remote
330	changes early.
331	
332	### .claude cadence
333	
334	The `.claude` working-copy node accumulates session data
335	across the cycle's commits and is described + committed
336	when a push happens — **one push, one `.claude` commit**,
337	paired with every code commit in that push. Its change
338	ID is stable from the first code commit that references
339	it through to that `.claude` commit (working-copy
340	snapshots, `jj describe`, and the finalize `jj commit`
341	all preserve it), so the `ochid:` trailers resolve
342	across the push.
343	
344	`.claude` is a linear journal — all session work lives
345	on `main`, regardless of the app-side bookmark. **Do not
346	create or maintain `.claude` bookmarks that mirror
347	app-side branches** — risks the bot steering session
348	pushes to the wrong remote ref.
349	
350	### Bot communication at the reviews
351	
352	Use plain prose — no insider jargon ("Gate N signal",
353	"Checkpoint N", etc.):
354	
355	- **At Work review** — summarize what changed and stop.
356	  "Work complete. Please review."
357	- **At Commit Description review** — present `$TITLE`
358	  and `$BODY` explicitly; ask permission to commit (or
359	  push). Don't spell out the full `vc-x1 push <bookmark>
360	  --yes --title "$TITLE" --body "$BODY"` invocation by
361	  default; it's mechanical and obvious from title/body.
362	- **At Post close-out review** — surface the shape
363	  options (squash / merge / keep) with a brief
364	  trade-off summary plus the push target; wait for the
365	  user's choice before any `jj squash` / `jj rebase` /
366	  `jj git push` invocation.
367	
368	### After finalize: stop and wait
369	
370	After `vc-x1 finalize` launches — whether mid-session
371	per-push or at session-end — you **MUST NEVER** proceed
372	to a next step, edit files, run tools, or emit any text
373	until the user explicitly directs you to continue.
374	**This holds even when the next step seems obvious —
375	wait.** Treat finalize as a hard stop for the whole
376	turn; any final words go in the approval prompt
377	*before* executing finalize. The user controls cadence —
378	auto-proceeding bypasses the push+finalize checkpoint.
379	
380	### Recovery
381	
382	- **If push exits before `finalize-claude`** (e.g.
383	  failure between `push-app` and `finalize-claude`), run
384	  finalize by hand:
385	
386	  ```
387	  vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
388	  ```
389	
390	- **Clear push's saved state** after any out-of-band
391	  recovery — `rm .vc-x1/push-state.toml` or `vc-x1 push
392	  <bookmark> --restart` — otherwise push resumes from a
393	  stale stage.
394	- **Late code-repo tweak after `push-app` succeeded**
395	  (e.g. updating CLAUDE.md or memory) requires `jj
396	  squash --ignore-immutable` and a re-push; that is a
397	  remote rewrite and needs explicit approval like any
398	  push.
399	
```

> AGENT

Yes, agreed. Where I see prose bloat in `## Pushing`:

1. **`### Shape at close-out push` intro paragraph** (5 lines → 3): "Decide the shape and get user approval before pushing — the choice is hard to undo. Surface the options, wait for the user." Drops the "treat like Work / Commit Description reviews" cross-reference (the rule is just "surface and wait" — same as any review).

2. **Squash bullet** (7 lines → 5): "single entry on the target" already implies per-commit granularity loss; "every" / "into one" implied. Drop the redundancies.

3. **Merge bullet** (8 lines → 6): "with parents `<prev>` and the cycle tip" is implied by "merge commit"; "as the merge's second parent" → "via the second parent"; minor squeezes.

4. **Trailing "If squashing or merging" paragraph** (3 lines → 2): reframe as "Set up squash/merge before invoking `vc-x1 push`; use `jj git push` directly for non-standard shapes."

5. **`### .claude cadence` first paragraph** (9 lines → 4-5): the cadence rule and the chid-stability mechanism are entangled. Split into a one-line rule + a short mechanism sentence:

   > **Cadence**: one push = one `.claude` commit, paired with every code commit in that push.
   >
   > The `.claude` working copy accumulates session data across the cycle; its change ID stays stable across snapshots, `jj describe`, and the finalize commit, so code-side `ochid:` trailers resolve.

6. **`### Bot communication > Commit Description review` bullet** (5 lines → 3-4): drop "it's mechanical and obvious from title/body" (redundant); abbreviate the example invocation.

7. **`### Bot communication > Post close-out review` bullet** (5 lines → 4): drop "with a brief trade-off summary plus the push target" (the bot can decide format).

8. **`### After finalize`** (10 lines → 7): tighten parenthetical "whether mid-session per-push or at session-end" → "(mid-session per-push or at session-end)"; consolidate the closing "controls cadence — auto-proceeding bypasses" into one sentence.

Net expected: ~130 lines → ~110 lines. About 20 lines of pure prose compression.

Apply on a new node? Or want to narrow the scope first (e.g., just the heavy hitters — Shape bullets + .claude cadence + After finalize)?

```
Move revisions to different parent(s)

This command moves revisions to different parent(s) while preserving the
changes (diff) in the revisions.

There are three different ways of specifying which revisions to rebase:

* `--source/-s` to rebase a revision and its descendants
* `--branch/-b` to rebase a whole branch, relative to the destination
* `--revisions/-r` to rebase the specified revisions without their
  descendants

If no option is specified, it defaults to `-b @`.

There are three different ways of specifying where the revisions should be
rebased to:

* `--onto/-o` to rebase the revisions onto the specified targets
* `--insert-after/-A` to rebase the revisions onto the specified targets and
  to rebase the targets' descendants onto the rebased revisions
* `--insert-before/-B` to rebase the revisions onto the specified targets'
  parents and to rebase the targets and their descendants onto the rebased
  revisions

See the sections below for details about the different ways of specifying
which revisions to rebase where.

If a working-copy revision gets abandoned, it will be given a new, empty
revision. This is true in general; it is not specific to this command.

### Specifying which revisions to rebase

With `--source/-s`, the command rebases the specified revision and its
descendants to the destination. For example, `jj rebase -s M -o O` would
transform your history like this (letters followed by an apostrophe are
post-rebase versions):

```text
O           N'
|           |
| N         M'
| |         |
| M         O
| |    =>   |
| | L       | L
| |/        | |
| K         | K
|/          |/
J           J
```
```

> AGENT

Confirmed via `jj rebase --help`: the canonical flag is `--onto/-o` now. `-d` still works (silent — no deprecation warning), but it's not in the help output. So switching to `--onto` is right.

**Why the command works (mechanism):**

- `-r <tip>` selects the cycle's close-out commit (the `X.Y.Z`) as the revision to rewrite.
- **Multiple `--onto` flags give the rewritten commit multiple parents** — i.e., they construct a merge.
- First `--onto <prev>` becomes the first parent → the main-line ancestry.
- Second `--onto <sub-tip>` becomes the second parent → the cycle's content chain stays reachable.

So `<tip>` morphs from a normal end-of-chain commit into a merge commit pointing at both `<prev>` and `<sub-tip>`. `main` advances to `<tip>`; the chain `<prev>+1 ... <sub-tip>` is preserved via the second-parent traversal. That's the trapezoidal shape.

**Proposed bullet** (~6 lines):

```
- **Merge non-ff** — `main` gains a merge commit
  (`X.Y.Z`); cycle commits stay reachable via the second
  parent. `jj log -r ..@ -n <N>` shows the trapezoidal
  shape. Set up: `jj rebase -r <tip> --onto <prev>
  --onto <sub-tip>` (two `--onto` → `<tip>` becomes a
  merge of `<prev>` + `<sub-tip>`).
```

One line more than the current 5, but it explains *why* the command produces a merge rather than relying on the reader to know jj's multi-target rebase semantic.

> AGENT

All four edits applied on the current `@`. Diff summary will show on `jj diff`.

**My one clarifying question on `<sub-tip> == <prev>`:**

I want to make sure I understand — in my model:

- `<prev>` = the previous cycle's close-out commit (where `main` was BEFORE this cycle started).
- `<sub-tip>` = the current cycle's content tip (the last Work commit, parent of `<tip>`).
- `<tip>` = this cycle's close-out commit (the bare `X.Y.Z`).

So for *this* cycle (0.59.0):
- `<prev>` = `qzpynrkk a199d062 (0.58.0)` (previous close-out, where main is now).
- `<sub-tip>` = `knvppzwq` (current `-1` work commit, the eventual content tip).
- `<tip>` = the not-yet-created `0.59.0` close-out commit.

In that model `<sub-tip>` and `<prev>` are different commits (separated by 3+ cycle commits). I can't find a typical case where they'd be equal — even a minimal cycle (`-0` then close-out) has `<sub-tip>` = the preparation commit and `<prev>` = previous close-out, which are distinct.

Did you mean something different by "sub-tip == prev in the typical case"? Possibilities I considered but couldn't reconcile:

- (a) A zero-work-commit cycle? Even then `<sub-tip>` = preparation ≠ `<prev>`.
- (b) Some specific shape I haven't seen?
- (c) The variable names overlap meaning in some way I'm missing?

If it turns out you meant something else and `<prev>` defined in the bullet is wrong, easy fix.

# Todo Backlog

 Non-prioritized `## Todo` entries — the long tail. When an
 entry becomes a priority, move it (and any refs it cites)
 to `notes/todo.md > ## Todo` and add a tier line under the
 right `### Pn` in `notes/todo.md > ## Priorities`.

 Same formatting rules as `notes/todo.md > ## Todo` — see
 `notes/README.md > ## Todo format`. Run
 `vc-x1 fix-todo --no-dry-run notes/todo-backlog.md` to
 renumber.

## Todo

1. **vc-x1 push: `--merge` flag (close-out shape).** Teach
   push to set up the non-FF merge ("trapezoid") shape
   itself instead of requiring the user to pre-rebase
   before invoking push. Sibling to `--squash` (#23);
   both are close-out shape choices made at push time.
   Dogfooded manually in `0.56.0`. [[1]]
   - Preparation (`-0`): open chores section; settle flag
     surface (`--merge` mutually exclusive with `--squash`
     / `--keep`?) and preflight checks (cycle tip is
     descendant of `<prev>`, not already a merge, sub-tip
     reachable from current bookmark, etc.).
   - Work-1 (`-1`): parse the flag through `PushArgs` →
     `PushParams`; reject mutually-exclusive combinations.
   - Work-2 (`-2`): implement the rebase setup inside
     push's state machine — locate `<tip>` and
     `<sub-tip>`, run the equivalent of `jj rebase -r
     <tip> -d <prev> -d <sub-tip>`; ensure the `.claude`
     commit emits a multi-line `ochid:` covering every
     sub-step in the merge.
   - Work-3 (`-3`): tests + dogfood on a real cycle.
   - Close-out: finalize chores; `## Done` entry; CLAUDE.md
     `### Pushing` text updated to describe the flag.
2. **Investigate `linkme` for subcommand registration.**
   Distributed-slice registry — each subcommand registers itself
   at link time; `main.rs` discovers them via the slice rather
   than matching a `Commands` enum. Reduces per-subcommand
   touchpoints from 3 (mod decl + enum variant + match arm) to 1
   (registration). Costs: loses compile-time exhaustiveness
   (missing registration = runtime gap); help-output ordering
   depends on link order unless sorted; macro-magic dependency.
   Revisit once the `0.50.0` trait sweep's per-arm cost has been
   felt under real "add a subcommand" load.
   <https://github.com/dtolnay/linkme>
3. **Investigate `inventory` as `linkme` alternative.** Same
   shape as `linkme` — runtime-iterable registry populated by
   `inventory::submit!` per subcommand. Trade-offs mirror
```

> AGENT

Re-proposing what I sketched earlier for `## ochid trailers` (we got sidetracked to Pushing before applying):

```
## ochid trailers

Each commit body carries `ochid:` trailer(s) —
workspace-root-relative paths linking it to its
counterpart(s) in the other repo. Asymmetric because
`.claude` commits once per push:

- **Code-side commits** each carry one
  `ochid: /.claude/<.claude-chid>` — the `.claude`
  working-copy change ID, stable until `.claude` is
  committed at push time.
- **The `.claude` commit** carries one
  `ochid: /<code-chid>` per code commit in that push: a
  single trailer for one code commit (the squashed
  case), or a multi-line list for several (see
  [`forks-multi-user.md`](forks-multi-user.md)).

Use `vc-x1 chid -s code,bot -L` to capture the change
IDs (first line app, second `.claude`).
```

~17 lines vs current 22.

Changes:
- **Merge path definition into intro** — current two sentences become one (the path-explanation collapses into a dash-clause).
- **"Asymmetric because"** instead of "Because" — makes the *purpose* of the lead-in explicit (the bullets are different shapes because of the once-per-push cadence).
- **Tighten the `.claude` bullet's trailing multi-ochid sentence** — same content, fewer words.

Note: ochid invariant + bot-side rules + squash-gating are still scoped to `-2` (per the In Progress plan), so this pass keeps the section descriptive of mechanics only.

> AGENT

> DEVELOPER

I don't understand "Asymmetric because .claude commits once per push", this is a choice based on which Shape we use on the close-out and in the Keep separate it's 1:1.

> AGENT

You're right — that framing was wrong. The asymmetry isn't a property of `.claude` always; it depends on the shape. In Keep separate (1 code commit per push) and Squash (1 squashed commit per push), it's 1:1 — no asymmetry. Only Merge non-ff close-out batches multiple code commits per push, producing the multi-line ochid case.

**Revised framing** — drop the "Asymmetric because" lead-in entirely; state the rules directly:

```
## ochid trailers

Each commit body carries `ochid:` trailer(s) —
workspace-root-relative paths linking it to its
counterpart(s) in the other repo:

- **Code-side commits** each carry one
  `ochid: /.claude/<.claude-chid>` — the `.claude`
  working-copy change ID, stable until `.claude` is
  committed at push time.
- **The `.claude` commit** (one per push) carries one
  `ochid: /<code-chid>` per code commit in that push.
  Single-line when the push has one code commit;
  multi-line when several (see
  [`forks-multi-user.md`](forks-multi-user.md)).

Use `vc-x1 chid -s code,bot -L` to capture the change
IDs (first line app, second `.claude`).
```

Same line count (~17), but:
- **Intro**: just defines what ochid trailers are; no claim about asymmetry.
- **`.claude` bullet**: explicitly says "(one per push)" — that's the only true asymmetry (one `.claude` commit per push regardless of code-commit count); the trailer-count behavior follows from there. Single-line vs multi-line described by the push's code-commit count, not attributed to a specific shape (avoids being wrong about Squash etc.).

```
# Forking and multi-user coordination

Captures design discussion about two related but separable
concerns:

1. **Forking** a dual-repo workspace — working on an older
   code-side base while keeping the bot side append-only.
2. **Multi-user collaboration** on the same dual-repo —
   multiple humans, multiple bot sessions, code-side merges.

Both surface the same underlying tension: the code repo has
the classic git/jj DAG (branches, merges, rebases), while the
bot repo is fundamentally an append-only journal of
conversations that should never be rewritten.

For the *content* of the bot repo (vendor identity, format
versioning, multi-bot conventions, vendor-subdir directory
layout), see the companion file
[`bot-data-formats.md`](bot-data-formats.md).
```

2. **"ladder item"** — overloaded with `## Sub-cycle ladders` usage. "Work commit" or "code commit" is more precise here.

Each commit body carries `ochid:` trailer(s) —
workspace-root-relative paths linking it to its
counterpart(s) in the other repo. See

- **Code-side commits** each carry one
  `ochid: /.claude/<.claude-chid>` — the `.claude`

  per code commit in that push. More than one occurs on
  Merge non-ff close-out (one ochid per Work commit in
  the cycle).

Yes, both terms are used throughout without being defined. `jj-revsets.md` covers chid ("permanent and doesn't change"); ochid is project-specific (this file is the canonical def).

**My recommendation**: define both in the intro of `## ochid trailers` (the natural anchor — "ochid" derives from "chid"). One sentence each, with `jj-revsets.md` cross-link for chid. Other sections that use "chid" stay implicit since the file is small enough that a reader can find the definition.

Draft (~3 added lines to the existing intro):

```
## ochid trailers

A **chid** is jj's change ID — a permanent identifier
that survives rebases and describes (see
[`jj-revsets.md`](jj-revsets.md)). An **ochid** trailer
on a commit body links it to its counterpart in the
other repo via that counterpart's chid, written as a
workspace-root-relative path. See
[`forks-multi-user.md`](forks-multi-user.md) for the
multi-line trailer design (multi-user / forking).

- **Code-side commits** ...
```

**Tradeoff**: 3 lines longer but the terms become self-defined (helpful for README.md or external readers). Alternative: a `## Glossary` section at the top of the file (chid + ochid + other terms) — more discoverable but adds a section to the TOC.

> AGENT

Squashed. `## Iterative work` is already quite tight (17 lines, current state above). Honest assessment: not much obvious bloat here.

A few micro-observations if you want any of them addressed:

1. **"target commit" not explicitly tied back** — the intro says "for a single commit" and step 1 says "on top of the target commit." A reader landing here cold might wonder which commit is "the target." Could clarify in step 1 as "on top of the commit you're iteratively editing." Cost: 4 more words.

2. **Step 4's clause** — `jj squash -R .` to fold into the target; jj auto-creates a new empty `@` for the next round." — the second clause is informational (the loop continuation behavior). Could tighten to "`jj squash -R .` folds into the target and creates a new empty `@`." Same info, less prose.

3. **Closing paragraph** — "Same jj mechanics as a [sub-cycle ladder](#sub-cycle-ladders), but at single-commit scope — the cycle's version suffix doesn't change." — useful contrast. No real cut available without losing meaning.

4. **The section's title** — "Iterative work" is good. Alternatives: "Iteration loop", "Incremental rounds". Current is fine.

If none of these feel worth touching, the section can stay as-is. Otherwise pick what to apply. Or skip ahead to the last big section (`## Sub-cycle ladders`, ~120 lines — the biggest remaining)?

Make the next round of changes.\n3. User reviews the round (see\n   [Reviewing changes](#reviewing-changes)).\n4. `jj squash -R .` folds into the target and creates a\n   new empty `@`.\n\nSame jj mechanics as a\n[sub-cycle ladder](#sub-cycle-ladders), but at\nsingle-commit scope — the cycle's version suffix\ndoesn't change."
}

420	## Sub-cycle ladders
421	
422	The local ladder of sub-cycle Work commits is typically
423	kept on its own jj `@` chain and **collapsed into the
424	sub-cycle's Close-out** before the parent cycle continues.
425	Ladder commits are scratch — they exist for review and
426	bisection during the sub-cycle and disappear into one
427	commit at sub-cycle close-out.
428	
429	### Per-Work-commit contract within a ladder
430	
431	For each Work commit in the ladder:
432	
433	1. `jj new -R .` — create a fresh empty `@`.
434	2. Do the commit's work on this `@`.
435	3. Run `cargo test --bins`. **Non-negotiable.** Build and
436	   clippy alone are not enough — past experience
437	   (0.41.1-6.5) had a regression introduced in an early
438	   ladder commit that didn't surface until a later one ran
439	   the full suite, by which point bisection cost was higher
440	   than it needed to be.
441	4. `jj describe -m "..." -m "..." -R .` — give it a working
442	   title and body. The title does **not** need a version
443	   suffix during the ladder; the sub-cycle Close-out collects
444	   every commit into one final commit carrying the
445	   `(X.Y.Z-N)` marker. Ladder titles are scratch — they
446	   never reach the remote.
447	
448	### Navigating the ladder
449	
450	Common moves while a ladder is active:
451	
452	- `jj log -r 'all()' -R .` — see the whole ladder at a
453	  glance.
454	- `jj edit -r <prefix> -R .` — jump `@` to any commit in
455	  the ladder by chid prefix. Useful for bisection: re-run
456	  the failing test at each candidate without manual
457	  checkout juggling.
458	- `jj edit @-- -R .` — quick-jump back two commits.
459	- `jj diff -r <chid> -R .` — review one commit's diff in
460	  isolation.
461	
462	Example using a revset with the `::` operator from the last
463	commit pushed to the remote:
464	
465	```
466	$ jj log -r lu::
467	○  pynknoqu wink@saville.com 2026-05-01 16:16:23 33612c81
468	│  docs: cycle protocol pointer
469	@  xqpnxtyu wink@saville.com 2026-05-01 16:16:23 f40c49a7
470	│  docs: cycle protocol formal protocol
471	○  lzqmlpvy wink@saville.com 2026-05-01 16:16:23 8c722f8c
472	│  docs: jj-revsets.md review fixes
473	○  wmmxkuoz wink@saville.com 2026-05-01 11:28:46 65a6705f
474	│  chore: scratch — jj-revsets learning + scaffolding
475	◆  lulqxovr wink@saville.com 2026-04-27 22:27:23 main 55eadc8e
476	│  docs: capture 0.41.1 init+clone redesign plan (0.42.0-4)
477	~
478	```
479	
480	Ladder work happens on whatever `@` is currently selected.
481	Use `jj edit -r <chid>` to switch to whichever ladder
482	commit you want to work on. Modifications to a selected
483	commit rewrite that commit in place; descendants auto-rebase.
484	
485	### Close-out: squash the ladder
486	
487	When all ladder Work commits are done and `cargo test --bins`
488	is green on the latest:
489	
490	```
491	jj squash --from "<base>..@-" --into @ -u -R .
492	```
493	
494	Where `<base>` is the parent of the **first** ladder
495	commit — typically the bookmark or commit the ladder
496	branched from. The `-u` flag (use destination message)
497	tells jj to keep `@`'s description and discard the source
498	descriptions; the intermediate titles served their purpose,
499	and `vc-x1 push` will overwrite `@`'s description anyway.
500	
501	After squash:
502	
503	- All ladder diffs are folded into `@`.
504	- The intermediate commits become empty and are
505	  abandoned automatically.
506	- History is linear: `<base> → @`.
507	
508	Then run `vc-x1 push <bookmark>` exactly as for any other
509	commit. Push's `commit-app` stage finalizes `@` with the
510	sub-cycle Close-out title/body and the proper `(X.Y.Z-N)`
511	version suffix.
512	
513	For N = 1 the close-out squash is a no-op (`base..@-` is
514	empty when `@-` is `<base>`); push the single commit
515	directly.
516	
517	### Recovery
518	
519	If a ladder commit goes wrong and you want to back out
520	without losing prior commits:
521	
522	- **Discard the current commit's work.** `jj abandon @ -R .`
523	  drops it and gives you a fresh empty `@` on the same
524	  parent. Earlier commits are untouched.
525	- **Edit an earlier commit.** `jj edit -r <chid> -R .`,
526	  make corrections, then `jj edit -r <last-ladder-chid>`
527	  to return. Descendants auto-rebase.
528	- **Discard the entire ladder.** `jj op log -R .` shows the
529	  op history; `jj op restore <op-id> -R .` reverts the repo
530	  to that point. This is a full undo — it removes *all*
531	  ladder work after the chosen op. Use only when you want
532	  to start over. **Not** a close-out recipe; the squash
533	  above is.
534	
535	### Example
536	
537	A 3-commit ladder from `main`, then close-out squash:
538	
539	```
540	jj new -R .                   # repeat per commit:
541	... edit files; cargo test --bins ...
542	jj describe -m "..." -R .
543	
544	jj squash --from "main..@-" --into @ -u -R .
545	```
546	
547	Result: linear `main → @` with all ladder diffs folded
548	into `@`; intermediate commits abandoned. Then
549	`vc-x1 push main …` per normal.
```

```
          Replaces clap's auto-version (which would exit after printing): the
          banner now rides along with normal subcommand execution rather than
          gating it, so scripts can capture the version *and* the command's
          output in one invocation.

  -R, --repo <PATH>
          Workspace root or single jj repo path [default: .]

  -v, --verbose...
          Verbose output: -v debug, -vv trace

      --log <LOG>
          Log file path (captures all levels)

  -s, --scope <code|bot|code,bot>
          Side(s) to query; composes with --repo as workspace root

  -n, --commits <COMMITS>
          Number of commits to show

  -l, --label <TEXT>
          Custom label decoration between repos
          
          [default: ===]

  -L, --no-label
          Suppress label between repos

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

**Commit operation**: since the content is in `@-` (not `@` — we iterated via `jj new` + `jj squash` rounds and `@` is now an empty commit), the right command is `jj describe -r @-` rather than `jj commit`:

```
jj describe -r @- -m "<title>" -m "<body>" -R .
```

This sets `@-`'s description without creating a new commit (the empty `@` above already exists from prior squashes). No push — `-1` stays local; we'll push later (interim or at close-out).

Approve the title + body + the describe approach? Or want changes?

Hmm, on the other hand, I now vote to close this out 0.59.0 and squash it, thoughts

> AGENT

Honest reconsideration — there's a real case for it:

**For squashing now:**
- `-1` already captures the cycle's bulk value (relocate + extensive tightening — most of what 0.59.0 was for).
- The `-2` codifications (ochid invariant, squash gating, bot-repo rules) are rules-for-the-future; they bind work that doesn't exist yet (symmetric-squash tooling). Deferring them to a future cycle has low cost since nothing currently depends on them.
- The chores relocation Idea is bounded but isn't blocking anything either.

**The catch (we discussed this earlier):**

`.claude/main`'s `af60f979` (the `0.59.0-0.1` push) has TWO ochid trailers pointing at app-side `vxrtvvrs` (0.59.0-0) and `yqzowtqv` (0.59.0-0.1). Squashing 0.59.0 makes those app chids unreachable from `main`. The cleanup options are still:

- **K. Keep `feat/0.59.0` on origin after the squash** — the pre-squash app chids stay reachable; trailers continue to resolve. Cost: a permanent feature branch on origin "abandoned relative to main." **My recommended choice** — cheapest and matches the comment already in `af60f979`'s body ("pushed app side to a feature branch to preserve squash candidacy for close-out").
- **D. Delete `feat/0.59.0`** — clean remote; trailers go dangling (resolve only locally via jj op log until GC).
- **F. Force-push `.claude/main` to rewrite `af60f979`** — replace the two trailers with one pointing at the squashed chid. Clean trailer state, but force-push and loses the per-commit granularity that the existing trailers preserve.

**Net thought**: yes, squash + Option K is reasonable. We end up with:
- `main` gets one clean `0.59.0` commit (the squash).
- `feat/0.59.0` lingers as the "long-way" record (preserves ochid resolution).
- Future cycles handle the codifications.
- The Ideas section + the chores-relocation Idea entry get carried into the squashed commit (so the Idea stays queued for a future cycle).

If you want to go with squash, **which cleanup — K, D, or F?** And do you want to drop the `-2 Refine` plan ladder entry from In Progress now (since it's becoming the close-out instead of a separate Work commit)?

> DEVELOPER

I think we should force-push the bot repo description and update the chid, this is something we need to handle, would you agree?

> AGENT

Agreed. Option F is the right thing — it respects the ochid invariant (even though we haven't codified it in cycle-protocol.md yet, doing F now sets the precedent for "this is how squash works properly"). The manual version IS what `vc-x1 push --squash` would automate later.

**Concrete steps to execute F:**

1. **Re-bump Cargo.toml** `0.59.0-2` → `0.59.0` (close-out version).
2. **Rewrite the In Progress block** to reflect the actual cycle (drop the `-2 Refine` plan items since `-2` isn't happening; just `-0`, `-0.1`, `-1`, `0.59.0` close-out).
3. **Capture the deferred work** (`-2`'s planned items) as Ideas / Todos so they don't get lost:
   - Ochid invariant codification + bot-repo rules + squash gating → `## Ideas` (or `## Todo` if you want them prioritized).
   - Symmetric-squash tooling → `## Todo` / `## Priorities` P1.
   - Ideas-aware Preparation/Close-out bullets → smaller `## Ideas` entry.
   - chores-12 design subsection updates → ?(part of next cycle's work either way).
4. **Apply close-out bookkeeping**: move picked-up item to `## Done`, move `## In Progress` block into chores-12 section, update `notes/README.md` if applicable.
5. **Commit close-out** as bare `0.59.0`.
6. **Squash cycle** (`-0`, `-0.1`, `-1`, `0.59.0`) into one commit on `main`. Capture the squashed commit's chid.
7. **Bot-side rewrite**: `jj describe -r af60f979` (or its chid `otywtnxx`) — replace the two existing `ochid:` trailers with one `ochid: /<squashed-chid>`. Verify `af60f979`'s chid (`otywtnxx`) stays stable through `jj describe`.
8. **Force-push `.claude/main`** (rewrites the published `af60f979` commit ID; the chid is preserved).
9. **Push app `main`** with the squashed commit.
10. **Then** finalize `.claude` for THIS push (the current session work since `af60f979` becomes a new bot commit, paired with the squashed app commit via `ochid: /<squashed-chid>`).

**Two things to flag:**

- **Force-pushing `.claude/main`** rewrites a public history — exactly the operation we want to teach the tool to do, but doing it by hand is a remote rewrite that warrants an explicit "are you sure" beat.
- **Setting precedent without codification**: we're doing F before the cycle-protocol.md says "do F when squashing." Future readers reconstructing the rule from this cycle's chores-12 narrative will see the precedent. That's fine but worth noting in the close-out chores section.

Confirm we should proceed with the 10-step plan above? Or want to discuss any step first?

> DEVELOPER

One thing, we're going to end up with two bot commits pointing to one code commit, I don't have a problem with that but want to make sure you agree.

> AGENT

Agreed — 2:1 bot→code is fine. Reasoning:

- **Semantically accurate**: both bot commits' session data DID contribute to the squashed code commit. `af60f979`'s session led to `-0`/`-0.1`; the new bot commit's session led to `-1` + close-out work. All collapsed into one code commit. So both bot commits honestly describe their session as contributing to that code commit.
- **Already in the protocol's design space**: the existing N:1 bot→code case (one bot commit, multiple ochid trailers for multiple code commits in one push) is the inverse asymmetry. Post-F, we have many bot commits → one code commit. Both are natural consequences of an append-only bot side paired with a code side that can squash.
- **Code→bot stays 1:1**: the squashed code commit's `ochid:` points at the new close-out bot commit (its actual push pair). The fact that `af60f979` ALSO claims association from the bot side doesn't break the code-side rule (each code commit carries one `ochid:` linking to ONE bot commit). The bot side's append-only journal carries the richer history.

**One suggestion worth flagging**: when rewriting `af60f979`'s description, consider including a brief note in the body acknowledging the rewrite — e.g., "Trailer rewritten 2026-05-25 when 0.59.0 squashed; originally paired with 0.59.0-0 and 0.59.0-0.1 (now collapsed)." That preserves the historical truth for future readers without losing the new (valid) ochid pointer.

Without that note, a reader sees `af60f979` (titled "chore: WIP session capture (0.59.0-0.1)") pointing at a `0.59.0` squashed commit, which feels like a versioning mismatch. The note explains.

Add the rewrite note, or keep `af60f979`'s rewrite minimal (just the trailer change)?

104	1. **vc-x1 push: support new cycle protocol shape (N:1 code↔bot).**
105	   Today push assumes 1:1 symmetric WC commits with shared
106	   title/body. The new cycle protocol has a different shape on
107	   each side:
108	   - code side: fully committed before push (N commits via
109	     merge), nothing to commit at push time
110	   - `.claude` side: one commit with its own message (distinct
111	     from any code commit) and a multi-line `ochid:` listing
112	     all N code commits
113	
114	   Teach push to:
115	   - detect this shape
116	   - skip `commit-app` when code WC is empty
117	   - compose a `.claude`-specific message
118	   - emit multi-line `ochid:` per the design in [[10]]
119	
120	   Today's workaround: pre-commit `.claude` manually, then
121	   `vc-x1 push <bm> --from bookmark-both --yes`.
122	2. **single-field `options_flags` leaves → `value` field.**
123	   `0.47.0` introduced the convention (single-field leaf names
124	   its field `value`, declares the flag via `#[arg(long = "…")]`,
125	   so consumers read `args.<leaf>.value` not `args.<leaf>.<leaf>`)
126	   on the new `squash` leaf. Sweep the pre-existing single-field
127	   leaves to match: `repo`, `dry_run`, `private`, `account`,
128	   `config`, `use_template` + their consumers
129	   (`init.rs`, tests).
130	
131	   Note: can a single field be defined as an type or enum instead
132	   of a struct and maybe eliminate the `args.<leaf>.<leaf>` name
133	   issue.
134	3. **`por → dual` conversion.** Attach a `.claude`
135	   companion repo + `.vc-config.toml` to an existing por
136	   workspace; emit cross-links going forward. Manual
137	   setup on an external por workspace (2026-05-14)
138	   proved arduous; this should be a routine subcommand.
139	   Design stub in [[1]] § 2.
140	4. **por/dual parity + `dual → por` conversion.** Make

144	   `--scope` rollout below. Pre-design; goal + open

Without this,\n   squash is a manual recipe that future cycles must\n   follow each time.\n   - App side: squash cycle commits into one new commit;\n     capture the squashed chid.\n   - Bot side: rewrite the prior push commit's\n     description — replace its per-commit `ochid:`\n     trailers with one pointing at the squashed chid;\n     add a rewrite-note acknowledging the change\n     (preserves historical truth for future readers).\n   - Force-push bot `main` (rewrites the published\n     commit; chid preserved via `jj describe`).\n   - Push app `main`. The new bot commit paired with\n     this push receives `ochid: /<squashed-chid>` as\n     normal — produces a 2:1 bot→code pattern that's\n     already in the protocol's design space.\n   - Gates `Squash to one commit` as a routine\n     close-out shape vs. the current manual recipe.\n3. **single-field `options_flags` leaves → `value` field.**"
}

# Notes

This directory contains various notes and documentation related to the project.
Each file is organized by topic for easy reference.

By default there are chores-*.md and todo.md. Chores are general notes
about tasks and todo.md contains short term tasks and their status.
The chores-NN files are numbered in sequence; older ones are closed
(chores-09.md and earlier — through the 0.48.x cycles), and the
active one ([chores-10.md](chores-10.md), the 0.49.0 cycle onward)
also carries the **refactor-tracking** tables (the Context+Params
port and the options_flags extraction).

App architecture — the CLI-args / `Context`+`Params` split, a
generic module map, the subcommand model, and what the
Context+Params port and the options_flags extraction *are* (the
*live status* is in chores-10.md) — lives at the repo root in
[`../ARCHITECTURE.md`](../ARCHITECTURE.md).

In the future we I expect we may want to create a "notes"
database to better manage the information, TBD.

Examples chore file:
```
# Chores-01.md
 
General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
how and why of a particular chore.
```

A little problem our current branch is feat/0.59.0 not main!

> AGENT

Right — important catch. The state after squash:

- **`main`**: locally at `qzpynrkk a199d062` (0.58.0 — hasn't moved).
- **`feat/0.59.0`**: locally at `qzpynrkk` (jj moved it down when the old `-0`/`-0.1`/`-1` commits got abandoned by the squash). Origin still has `feat/0.59.0` at `yqzowtqv bc849e53` (the old `-0.1`), now "hidden" locally.
- **`nkorqyuz` (squashed close-out)**: not pointed at by any bookmark.

So jj created the squashed commit but didn't auto-advance any bookmark — that's our explicit move. The question is **which bookmark**:

**Option 1: Advance `main` → squashed.** The 0.59.0 deliverable lands on main directly. `feat/0.59.0` is a leftover that we can either:
- Delete from origin (clean — `jj git push --delete feat/0.59.0`).
- Leave on origin (Option K-style preservation — the pre-squash app chids stay reachable through that branch, though we already chose Option F for the bot side).

**Option 2: Advance `feat/0.59.0` → squashed (don't touch main).** Keep the cycle on the feature branch; merge to main later as a separate step. Doesn't match the "close out 0.59.0" intent.

For Option F + squash to main, **Option 1 with `feat/0.59.0` deleted from origin** is the cleanest end state (Option F handles the ochid invariant; we don't need K's branch preservation).

Concretely:
```
jj bookmark move main --to @ -R .
jj git push --delete feat/0.59.0 -R .   # after main is pushed
```

But first, before any of these moves, I should also verify: do we want to **delete `feat/0.59.0`** as part of this push, or keep it on origin?

Confirm: advance `main` to `@`, then proceed with bot rewrite + force-push + push app main + delete `feat/0.59.0`?
