> AGENT

That's actually expected — jj's "working copy" terminology is misleading vs git. In jj, `@` IS a real commit (the close-out commit `nkorqyuz 8666657e "docs: extract cycle protocol (0.59.0)"`). What `jj st` shows as "Working copy changes" is the diff `@ vs @-` — i.e., the cumulative content of the squashed close-out commit relative to its parent (main / 0.58.0). All those files (CLAUDE.md, cycle-protocol.md, substep-protocol.md deletion, etc.) are inside `@`, not floating outside as uncommitted git-style edits.

Verification:
- `jj show -r @` would show the full description + the same file list (as the commit's content).
- The commit IS `nkorqyuz 8666657e` per the log line.

> AGENT

Yes, exactly — that's the post-amend `jj new` case from CLAUDE.md > `## jj Basics`. In a colocated jj+git repo, after `jj squash --into @`, `@` stays on the rewritten commit and git detaches HEAD at the commit's first parent, making git see the commit's content as "uncommitted relative to HEAD". `jj new` creates an empty `@` above so HEAD attaches cleanly to `nkorqyuz`.

```
Push to a Git remote

By default, pushes tracking bookmarks pointing to `remote_bookmarks(remote=<remote>)..@`. Use
`--bookmark` to push specific bookmarks. Use `--all` to push all bookmarks. Use `--change` to
generate bookmark names based on the change IDs of specific commits.

When pushing a bookmark, the command pushes all commits in the range from the remote's current
position up to and including the bookmark's target commit. Any descendant commits beyond the
bookmark are not pushed.

If the local bookmark has changed from the last fetch, push will update the remote bookmark to the
new position after passing safety checks. This is similar to `git push --force-with-lease` - the
remote is updated only if its current state matches what Jujutsu last fetched.

Unlike in Git, the remote to push to is not derived from the tracked remote bookmarks. Use
`--remote` to select the remote Git repository by name. There is no option to push to multiple
remotes.

Before the command actually moves, creates, or deletes a remote bookmark, it makes several [safety
checks]. If there is a problem, you may need to run `jj git fetch --remote <remote name>` and/or
resolve some [bookmark conflicts].

[safety checks]: https://docs.jj-vcs.dev/latest/bookmarks/#pushing-bookmarks-safety-checks

[bookmark conflicts]: https://docs.jj-vcs.dev/latest/bookmarks/#conflicts

Usage: jj git push [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --remote <REMOTE>
          The remote to push to (only named remotes are supported)
          
          This defaults to the `git.push` setting. If that is not configured, and if there are
          multiple remotes, the remote named "origin" will be used.

  -b, --bookmark <BOOKMARK>
          Push only this bookmark, or bookmarks matching a pattern (can be repeated)
          
          If a bookmark isn't tracking anything yet, the remote bookmark will be tracked
          automatically.
          
          By default, the specified pattern matches bookmark names with glob syntax. You can also
          use other [string pattern syntax].
          
          [string pattern syntax]: https://docs.jj-vcs.dev/latest/revsets/#string-patterns

      --all
          Push all bookmarks (including new bookmarks)

      --tracked
```

> AGENT

`jj git push` uses force-with-lease by default — the rewrite of `af60f979` will push automatically (no `--force` flag needed) as long as origin hasn't moved since our last fetch. Good.

For `vc-x1 finalize`: it handles the standard "describe @ + advance bookmark + push" flow, but doesn't know about rewriting an existing committed commit (the `af60f979` description rewrite). So the sequence is:

**Manual step (only thing finalize doesn't cover):**
1. `jj describe -R .claude -r otywtnxx` — rewrite `af60f979`'s description.

**Standard finalize:**
2. `jj describe -R .claude -r @` — set the bot WC's description (this becomes the close-out push's bot commit).
3. `vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --push main --delay 0` — advance bot main bookmark to `@`, push (which also publishes the rewritten `af60f979`).

**App push:**
4. `vc-x1 finalize --repo . --push main --delay 0` (or `jj git push --bookmark main` directly).

**Cleanup:**
5. `jj git push --delete feat/0.59.0` to remove the feature branch from origin.

Good clarification — that reframes the rule: bot squash is normal (every push), bot merge across sessions is forbidden. The `af60f979` rewrite + the new bot WC commit are both "describe" operations (preserve chids), well within the actual rule.

The "what session this was" info lives in the .jsonl files, not the description.

- **B (af60f979 keeps its identity): `af60f979` keeps its historical title `chore: WIP session capture (0.59.0-0.1)` + original body + rewrite note + updated ochid; only the new bot WC matches the squashed code's title/body.** Bot history reads as a journey (each commit narrates its own session); trailers are valid post-squash.

I lean **B** because it preserves the bot's journey narrative — a reader scrolling through bot history sees distinct commits for distinct sessions. **A** is cleaner for "trailer alignment" but loses the per-session narrative; the .jsonl files become the only differentiator.
